`copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_return_materialization(...)` helper plus a narrow return-materialization surface so outcome-selection metadata can flow through one scaffold-only success/fallback native return-statement materialization path before return emission instead of leaving return-materialization execution implicit, the return-emission layer now also emits a shared `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_return_emission(...)` helper plus a narrow return-emission surface so return-materialization metadata can flow through one scaffold-only emitted-branch-block path before final-return adoption instead of leaving return-emission execution implicit, the final-return-adoption layer now also emits a shared `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_final_return_adoption(...)` helper plus a narrow final-return-adoption surface so return-emission metadata can flow through one scaffold-only adopted-return-block path before return activation instead of leaving final-return-adoption execution implicit, the return-activation layer now also emits a shared `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_return_activation(...)` helper plus a narrow return-activation surface so final-return-adoption metadata can flow through one scaffold-only active/deferred return-selection path before stub-return handling instead of leaving return-activation execution implicit, the stub-return layer now also emits a shared `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_stub_return(...)` helper plus a narrow stub-return surface so return-activation metadata can flow through one scaffold-only placeholder/deferred stub-return path before placeholder-return-value handling instead of leaving stub-return execution implicit, the placeholder-return-value layer now also emits a shared `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_placeholder_return_value(...)` helper plus a narrow placeholder-return-value surface so stub-return metadata can flow through one scaffold-only placeholder-return contract before final DLL/FLL stub emission instead of leaving placeholder-return-value execution implicit, the final stub-emission layer now also emits a shared `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_placeholder_return_int(...)` helper so final placeholder integer selection flows through one scaffold-only helper before DLL direct return or FLL `_RetInt(...)` surface application instead of staying inline inside the output-specific stub emitters, the shared parsed-int helper layer now also emits the current default integer sentinel through a shared helper instead of repeating the raw inline `-1` token inside the parser, the response-artifact layer now also emits the current empty response-document token through a shared helper instead of repeating the raw inline empty-string literal, the payload layer now emits the current request/response shape contracts plus the current request/response field-name contracts through shared helpers instead of repeating those raw inline tokens, the payload and interpretation layers now emit the current response `status`, `return_value`, and `diagnostics` field-name contracts through shared helpers instead of repeating those raw inline tokens across multiple builders, the failure-policy layer now records non-zero-exit handling, missing-response handling, a deterministic `runtime_host_failure` diagnostics fallback, and the placeholder return fallback value for each stub, and it now emits that diagnostics fallback through a shared helper instead of repeating the raw token inline, the response-validation layer records the expected response media type, schema version, required response fields, and the success-status token expected by the wrapper scaffold before it would accept a host response, and it now emits that success-status token through a shared helper instead of repeating the raw token inline, the request-artifact layer records the serialized request document that would be written to the planned request-path artifact, the request-write layer records the concrete target request path plus overwrite policy that would be used before launch, the response-read layer records the concrete staged response path plus text-read policy that would be used after launch, the response-artifact layer records the raw staged response document payload before any parsing or interpretation, the response-parse layer records the parser kind plus status/value/diagnostics field extraction contract that would be applied to that raw document, the interpreted-result layer records the combined success-status, wrapper-return-surface, and fallback-return contract that would drive the eventual native return path, the native-return layer now also records typed success and fallback integer values alongside the success/fallback native value representations plus final DLL/FLL return surface that would eventually materialize from that interpreted result, reusing `copperfin_parse_runtime_bridge_int_value_representation(...)` to derive those typed values from the existing success/fallback representation metadata instead of hardcoding them again, the return-materialization layer now also emits a shared native-`int` return-surface helper so the current plain-`int` branch no longer repeats that raw inline token, a shared native-`int` placeholder-signature helper so typed `_RetInt(int)`-style matching no longer repeats the raw inline `"(int)"` token, and a shared native return-statement framing helper so the plain-`int`, typed placeholder, and representation fallback branches now all reuse one `return ...;` contract instead of repeating the raw framing tokens before the function falls back to representation-based materialization for any non-`int` surfaces, the outcome-selection layer records the explicit success/fallback branch conditions plus diagnostics field that would govern which native return path is chosen, the return-materialization layer now routes the current `int` / `_RetInt(int)` success/fallback statements through those parsed typed integer values while preserving the existing representation-based fallback path for any non-`int` surfaces, the return-emission layer now records an explicit fallback else-branch statement and composes the emitted return block from the prebuilt success/fallback branch statements instead of re-materializing the fallback return inline, the final-return-adoption layer records how that emitted return block would replace the current placeholder native return in the generated wrapper stub, the return-activation layer now also seeds the future `active_return_block` from that adopted return block while keeping activation disabled so the scaffold still emits the placeholder return today, the stub-return layer now routes its inactive `deferred_return_block` through `return_activation_plan.active_return_block` instead of reaching back to final-adoption metadata, explicitly carries the placeholder fallback integer/value metadata needed by the current helper path, flattens the upstream `activation_mode` / `adoption_mode` contracts for downstream consumption, derives explicit placeholder-helper active/replacement policy booleans from that upstream metadata, and now also emits shared placeholder return-binding and placeholder return-statement helpers so exported stubs, failure-policy fallback metadata, and final-return adoption all consume one shared placeholder return contract instead of repeating raw inline `-1` binding and return-statement literals, while the placeholder-return-value layer carries those activation/adoption mode contracts plus the derived helper-policy booleans alongside the deferred return block, placeholder-emission flag, emitted placeholder return statement, and fallback value metadata so the current helper consumes the full plan-level activation/deferred/emitted/helper-policy contract instead of reconstructing the same policy from string comparisons in the helper body, and the host-toolchain compile check matches the emitted CMake visibility policy so wrapper build checks track only the deliberate callable/data export surface
- Native-wrapper stub-emission admission notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also emit a shared `copperfin_runtime_bridge_admit_stub_emission(...)` helper plus a narrow `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeStubEmissionAdmission` surface so staged placeholder-return-int admission output and final stub-emission contracts flow through one scaffold-only helper before output-specific stub emission instead of remaining implicit around the final stub-emission seam
- Native-wrapper stub-emission execution notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also emit a shared `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_stub_emission(...)` helper plus a narrow `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeStubEmission` surface so staged placeholder-return-value execution and placeholder-return-int execution flow through one scaffold-only helper before DLL/FLL output-specific stub emission instead of remaining implicit across the final stub-emission execution seam
- Native-wrapper stub-emission return-surface notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also emit a shared `copperfin_runtime_bridge_build_stub_emission_return_surface(...)` helper plus a narrow `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeStubEmissionReturnSurface` surface so shared stub-emission execution output flows through one scaffold-only helper before DLL/FLL output-specific stub return application instead of remaining implicit across the final stub-emission return-surface seam
- Native-wrapper stub-emission output-application notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also emit a shared `copperfin_runtime_bridge_apply_stub_emission_output(...)` helper so shared stub-emission return-surface output flows through one scaffold-only helper before the final DLL/FLL return adapter is applied instead of remaining implicit inside the output-specific stub emitters
- Native-wrapper stub-emission emitter notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also emit a shared `copperfin_runtime_bridge_emit_stub_return_shared(...)` helper so the formerly duplicated DLL/FLL `copperfin_runtime_bridge_emit_stub_return(...)` bodies now flow through one scaffold-only emitter helper before output-specific wrapper forwarding instead of remaining duplicated across the two output-specific emitters
- Native-wrapper stub-emission wrapper notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also emit a shared `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeStubEmissionWrapper` surface plus `copperfin_runtime_bridge_build_stub_emission_wrapper(...)` so the remaining output-specific `copperfin_runtime_bridge_emit_stub_return(...)` bodies now only construct one wrapper contract before handing off to the shared stub-emission emitter helper instead of forwarding raw return-surface and adapter inputs directly
- Native-wrapper stub-emission wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgePlaceholderReturnValuePlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the placeholder-return-value plan and then route placeholder returns straight through `copperfin_runtime_bridge_emit_stub_return_shared(...)` instead of keeping separate output-specific stub-return wrapper helpers
- Native-wrapper stub-return wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeStubReturnPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the stub-return plan and then build placeholder-return-value plans straight from the enriched stub-return plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the placeholder-plan seam
- Native-wrapper return-activation wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeReturnActivationPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the return-activation plan and then build stub-return plans straight from the enriched activation plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the stub-return-plan seam
- Native-wrapper final-return-adoption wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeFinalReturnAdoptionPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the final-return-adoption plan and then build return-activation plans straight from the enriched final-return-adoption plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the return-activation-plan seam
- Native-wrapper return-emission wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeReturnEmissionPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the return-emission plan and then build final-return-adoption plans straight from the enriched return-emission plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the final-return-adoption-plan seam
- Native-wrapper return-materialization wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeReturnMaterializationPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the return-materialization plan and then build return-emission plans straight from the enriched return-materialization plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the return-emission-plan seam
- Native-wrapper outcome-selection wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeOutcomeSelectionPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the outcome-selection plan and then build return-materialization plans straight from the enriched outcome-selection plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the return-materialization-plan seam
- Native-wrapper native-return wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeNativeReturnPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the native-return plan and then build outcome-selection plans straight from the enriched native-return plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the outcome-selection-plan seam
- Native-wrapper interpreted-result wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeInterpretedResultPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the interpreted-result plan and then build native-return plans straight from the enriched interpreted-result plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the native-return-plan seam
- Native-wrapper response-parse wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeResponseParsePlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the response-parse plan and then build interpreted-result plans straight from the enriched response-parse plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the interpreted-result-plan seam
- Native-wrapper response-artifact wrapper notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeResponseArtifact`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the response artifact and then build response-parse plans straight from the enriched response artifact instead of passing the wrapper separately at the response-parse-plan seam
- Native-wrapper response-read wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeResponseReadPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the response-read plan and then build response artifacts straight from the enriched response-read plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the response-artifact seam
- Native-wrapper request-write wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeRequestWritePlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the request-write plan and then build response-read plans straight from the enriched request-write plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the response-read-plan seam
- Native-wrapper request-artifact wrapper notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeRequestArtifact`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the request artifact and then build request-write plans straight from the enriched request artifact instead of passing the wrapper separately at the request-write-plan seam
- Native-wrapper bridge response-contract expectation notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper request JSON now carries expected response media type plus a `response_fields` array serialized from `CopperfinRuntimeBridgePayloadPlan::response_fields`, so runtime-host bridge consumers can produce and validate the matching response envelope from the same wrapper request document
- Native-wrapper response-document validation-routing notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now pass the executed `response_document` into `copperfin_runtime_bridge_evaluate_response_validation(...)`, and `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeResponseValidationEvaluation` records response-document availability from the response body so response-contract enforcement has the actual bridge payload at the validation seam
- Native-wrapper required response-field validation notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper response validation now checks `response_document` for every field declared by `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeResponseValidationPlan::required_response_fields` and routes to the existing fallback return path when any required response field is absent
- Native-wrapper response media-type validation notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper response validation now extracts the response document's `response_media_type` field and compares it with `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeResponseValidationPlan::expected_response_media_type`, routing to the existing fallback return path when the field is absent or mismatched
- Native-wrapper response schema-version validation notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper response-field contracts now include `schema_version`, and response validation compares the response document's schema-version field with `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeResponseValidationPlan::expected_schema_version`, routing to the existing fallback return path when the field is absent or mismatched
- Native-wrapper runtime-host dispatch execution notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper launch helpers now quote and build a runtime-host command line from `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeDispatchExecution`, execute it through `std::system(...)`, capture the observed exit code, and mark launch success only when that exit code matches the expected exit code instead of returning deterministic launch failure
- Native-wrapper launch-environment dispatch notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper dispatch execution now carries `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeLaunchPlan::environment` into command construction, and runtime-host command lines now apply those environment assignments before launching the host
- Native-wrapper missing-response document-evaluation notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now pass the executed `response_document` into `copperfin_runtime_bridge_evaluate_missing_response(...)`, and missing-response policy treats the response as missing only when an existing response is required and the read document is empty
- Native-wrapper bridge request-field serialization notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper request JSON now emits a `request_fields` array serialized from `CopperfinRuntimeBridgePayloadPlan::request_fields`, so runtime-host bridge consumers can inspect the exact request contract carried by the wrapper request document
- Native-wrapper bridge request-field contract notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper `CopperfinRuntimeBridgePayloadPlan::request_fields` now declares the same descriptor metadata fields serialized by the bridge request document, so the request-field contract stays synchronized with routine kind, source provenance, parameter declaration style, parameter-name, schema-version, export, arity, media-type, and parameter payload fields
- Studio builder/wizard registry notes — Studio now exposes a context-aware VFP-compatible builder and wizard registry for form, class, control, report, label, project, and data-environment designer surfaces, preserving `builder.app`, `ReportBuilder.app`, and `Wizards` equivalence metadata while giving modern designer shells stable Copperfin entry-point tokens and per-context launch, invocation-admission, dispatch, and injected execution seams to filter and run relevant actions by context; dispatch catalogs now route through the shared admission catalog seam, `copperfin_studio_host --builder-launch-catalog` plus `--builder-invocation-admission-catalog` expose the non-executing launch/admission catalogs as JSON without opening UI, spawning builders, or mutating assets, and `copperfin_studio_host --builder-execute` exposes explicitly admitted builder/wizard dispatch execution with launch-command, executed-command, observed-exit-code, and no-stale-metadata failure reporting. The shared VSIX/standalone Builders pane now invokes that contract only through the explicit `COPPERFIN_BUILDER_LAUNCH_COMMAND` configuration; absent configuration remains a localized no-spawn boundary.
- Studio toolbox palette notes — Studio now exposes a VFP-compatible toolbox palette registry for common form, class, container, and report designer controls, including VFP class/baseclass metadata, default object-name prefixes, container flags, context filtering, and non-mutating launch catalogs across known Studio selection contexts so modern shells can drive toolbox creation without hardcoded UI-only control lists; toolbox dispatch now also has an injected execution seam with explicit admission, item metadata validation, launch observation, executed-state, and no-stale-metadata failure reporting; `copperfin_studio_host --toolbox-palette-launch-catalog` exposes the launch catalog as JSON without opening UI, spawning processes, creating controls, or mutating assets, and `copperfin_studio_host --toolbox-execute` exposes explicitly admitted toolbox dispatch execution with launch-command, executed-command, observed-exit-code, item metadata, and failure reporting.
- Studio context-editor action notes — Studio now exposes selection-context action metadata, non-executing launch catalogs, invocation-admission catalogs, dispatch catalogs, aggregate designer catalogs, toolbox palette admission catalogs, and toolbox dispatch catalogs for property-grid, method-editor, expression-editor, builder, toolbox, data-environment, and project-navigation surfaces so designer hosts can choose relevant editor commands for visual objects, report expressions, project items, and data-environment selections without hardcoded UI conditionals; editor-action dispatch now also has an injected execution seam with explicit admission, launch observation, executed-state, and no-stale-metadata failure reporting; `copperfin_studio_host --editor-action-launch-catalog`, `--editor-action-invocation-admission-catalog`, `--editor-action-execute`, and `--toolbox-invocation-admission-catalog` expose selected catalogs or admitted execution results as JSON.
- Studio designer-context aggregation notes — Studio now exposes a single active-selection context query that combines the context-aware editor action registry, builder/wizard registry, and toolbox palette registry so hosts can populate designer panes and commands from one filtered model for visual objects, report expressions, project items, and data-environment selections; aggregate designer dispatch now also has an execution seam that composes editor-action, builder, and toolbox execution with strict aggregate preflight so invalid aggregate inputs cannot launch partial child executions, `copperfin_studio_host --designer-execute` exposes that admitted aggregate execution path as JSON with child identity metadata, child dispatch argument arrays, child execution-state metadata, child process summaries, and exact child executed-command strings, and both `copperfin_studio_host --designer-dispatch-catalog` and `--designer-dispatch-execution-catalog` preserve nested child dispatch command tokens and dispatch arguments for non-mutating aggregate inspection.
- Studio selection-context builder launch/admission/dispatch notes — Studio now exposes non-executing builder/wizard launch, invocation-admission, and dispatch catalogs for one selected Studio context, so visual-object selections can preserve mixed form/control builder metadata and menu/data-environment selections can expose their context-specific builders without opening UI, spawning builders, or mutating assets; `copperfin_studio_host --selection-builder-launch-catalog`, `--selection-builder-invocation-admission-catalog`, and `--selection-builder-dispatch-catalog` expose the selection-scoped catalogs as JSON.
- Studio opened-document designer-context notes — opened Studio document models now carry default designer-context aggregates derived from asset kind, so form/class, report/label, project, and table/database-container assets expose relevant editor actions, builder/wizard entries, and toolbox entries directly from `open_document`.
- Studio host designer-context JSON notes — `copperfin_studio_host --json` now serializes opened-document designer contexts with stable selection-context tokens, editor action descriptors, builder/wizard descriptors, and toolbox item descriptors so external Studio and Visual Studio shells can consume the same context-aware model as in-process hosts.
- Studio host report-layout provenance JSON notes — `copperfin_studio_host --json` now serializes report/label layout provenance from `StudioReportLayoutSnapshot`, including document-title provenance, live/deleted setting/section/object counts, deleted collections, section/object raw codes, field ordinals, memo block numbers, memo-line indexes, highlight provenance, and unplaced-object counts for external report and label designers.
- Studio host selected report-section JSON notes — `copperfin_studio_host --json --record <section-record>` now exposes `selectedReportSectionAvailable` plus a provenance-rich `selectedReportSection` when the active report/label selection is a band record, and reports false/null for non-section selections.
- Studio host selected report-object JSON notes — `copperfin_studio_host --json --record <layout-object-record>` now exposes `selectedReportObjectAvailable` plus a provenance-rich `selectedReportObject` when the active report/label selection is a layout object record, and reports false/null for non-object selections.
- Studio host selected report-object section JSON notes — `copperfin_studio_host --json --record <layout-object-record>` now exposes `selectedReportObjectSectionAvailable` plus a provenance-rich `selectedReportObjectSection` for section-contained report/label layout objects, and reports false/null for unplaced, deleted, non-object, or unavailable selections.
- Studio host selected report-settings JSON notes — `copperfin_studio_host --json --record <root-settings-record>` now exposes `selectedReportSettingsAvailable` plus provenance-rich `selectedReportSettings` for report/label root settings, and reports false/null for non-settings selections.
- Studio host report-selection kind JSON notes — `copperfin_studio_host --json` now exposes `selectedReportSelectionAvailable` and `selectedReportSelectionKind` (`settings`, `section`, `object`, or `none`) so report/label designer hosts can branch on the active selection without probing each selected report payload.
- Studio host report printer-identity summary JSON notes — `copperfin_studio_host --json` now exposes additive `driverAvailable`/`driver`, `deviceAvailable`/`device`, and `outputAvailable`/`output` fields in the shared report-layout summary for FRX/LBX root settings, so hosts can consume printer identity without re-scanning raw settings arrays.
- Studio report object containing-section metadata notes — report/label layout objects now carry containing section id, containing section record index, and section-relative top coordinates in the core report layout model and Studio host JSON, with explicit null/missing metadata for unplaced or deleted objects.
- Studio report object section-order metadata notes — report/label layout objects now carry zero-based object order and object count within their containing section in the core report layout model and Studio host JSON, with explicit null/missing order metadata for unplaced or deleted objects.
- Studio report section order metadata notes — report/label sections now carry zero-based section order and live section count in the core report layout model, full Studio host report-layout JSON, and selected-section JSON, with explicit null/missing order metadata for deleted sections.
- Studio report layout bottom-edge metadata notes — report/label sections and layout objects now carry explicit bottom-edge coordinates in the core report layout model and Studio host JSON, including section-relative object bottom coordinates for section-contained objects while unplaced or deleted objects avoid fabricating relative section bottoms.
- Studio report layout right-edge metadata notes — report/label layout objects now carry explicit right-edge coordinates in the core report layout model and Studio host JSON so designer clients can consume full horizontal object spans without recomputing from left and width fields.
- Studio report layout nudge mutation notes — Studio host `--nudge-object` now has regression coverage proving stable-selector mutation of FRX report layout object `HPOS`/`VPOS` fields and refreshed report-layout JSON recomputation of absolute, edge, section-relative, and containing-section geometry after the mutation.
- Studio report layout alignment mutation notes — Studio host `--align-object --alignment-mode left` now has regression coverage proving stable-selector alignment of FRX report layout object `HPOS` fields and refreshed report-layout JSON recomputation of horizontal geometry after the mutation while preserving vertical and containing-section metadata.
- Studio report layout resize mutation notes — Studio host `--resize-object --resize-mode size` now has regression coverage proving stable-selector resizing of FRX report layout object `WIDTH`/`HEIGHT` fields and refreshed report-layout JSON recomputation of object spans after the mutation while preserving position and containing-section metadata.
- Studio report layout snap-to-grid mutation notes — Studio host `--snap-object --snap-mode both` now has regression coverage proving stable-selector grid snapping of FRX report layout object `HPOS`/`VPOS` fields and refreshed report-layout JSON recomputation of absolute, edge, section-relative, and containing-section geometry after the mutation.
- Studio report layout delete-state mutation notes — Studio host `--delete-object` now has regression coverage proving stable-selector deletion of FRX report layout object records and refreshed report-layout JSON movement from live section membership into deleted report objects while preserving selected-object metadata and false/null containing-section metadata.
- Studio report layout restore mutation notes — Studio host `--restore-object` now has regression coverage proving stable-selector restoration of deleted FRX report layout object records and refreshed report-layout JSON movement back into live section membership with selected-object and selected containing-section metadata available again.
- Studio deleted report layout object expression edit notes — Studio host `--set-property --record <deleted-layout-object-record> --property-name EXPR` now has regression coverage proving deleted FRX/LBX layout-object expression memo edits persist, refresh deleted-object and selected deleted-object expression metadata, and keep containing-section metadata unavailable/null instead of fabricating live section membership.
- Studio deleted report layout object expression clear notes — Studio host `--clear-property --record <deleted-layout-object-record> --property-name EXPR` now has regression coverage proving deleted FRX/LBX layout-object expression clears blank the memo property, refresh deleted-object and selected deleted-object expression metadata with fallback titles, and keep containing-section metadata unavailable/null instead of fabricating live section membership.
- Studio deleted report layout object font edit notes — Studio host `--set-property --record <deleted-layout-object-record> --property-name FONTFACE` now has regression coverage proving deleted FRX/LBX layout-object font memo edits persist, refresh deleted-object and selected deleted-object highlight metadata, and keep containing-section metadata unavailable/null instead of fabricating live section membership.
- Studio deleted report layout object font clear notes — Studio host `--clear-property --record <deleted-layout-object-record> --property-name FONTFACE` now has regression coverage proving deleted FRX/LBX layout-object font clears remove seeded font highlights, refresh deleted-object and selected deleted-object highlight metadata, and keep containing-section metadata unavailable/null instead of fabricating live section membership.
- Studio deleted report layout object width edit notes — Studio host `--set-property --record <deleted-layout-object-record> --property-name WIDTH` now has regression coverage proving deleted FRX/LBX layout-object width edits persist, refresh deleted-object and selected deleted-object right-edge geometry, and keep containing-section metadata unavailable/null instead of fabricating live section membership.
- Studio deleted report layout object width clear notes — Studio host `--clear-property --record <deleted-layout-object-record> --property-name WIDTH` now has regression coverage proving deleted FRX/LBX layout-object width clears recompute right-edge geometry, refresh deleted-object and selected deleted-object geometry metadata, and keep containing-section metadata unavailable/null instead of fabricating live section membership.
- Studio report layout distribution mutation notes — Studio host `--distribute-object --distribution-mode horizontal` now has regression coverage proving stable-selector redistribution of FRX report layout object `HPOS` fields and refreshed report-layout JSON recomputation of object coordinates while preserving containing-section and section object order/count metadata.
- Studio report layout reorder mutation notes — Studio host `--reorder-object --placement before` now has regression coverage proving stable-selector reorder of FRX report layout object records and refreshed report-layout JSON recomputation of section object indexes for tied-geometry objects.
- Studio report layout duplicate mutation notes — Studio host `--duplicate-object` now has regression coverage proving stable-selector duplication of FRX report layout object records, replacement `UNIQUEID` persistence, and refreshed report-layout JSON membership/index/count metadata for the appended copy.
- Studio report layout rename identity mutation notes — Studio host `--rename-object` now has regression coverage proving stable-selector replacement of FRX report layout object `UNIQUEID` values while preserving record order and refreshed live section membership/index/count metadata.
- Studio report section delete-state mutation notes — Studio host `--delete-object --record <section-record>` now has regression coverage proving FRX section-row delete flags persist and refreshed report-layout JSON moves the section to deleted-section metadata while leaving former section objects unplaced instead of fabricating containing sections.
- Studio report section restore mutation notes — Studio host `--restore-object --record <section-record>` now has regression coverage proving deleted FRX section rows can be restored, refreshed report-layout JSON returns them to live section metadata, and former section objects regain containing-section membership.
- Studio report settings delete-state mutation notes — Studio host `--delete-object --record <settings-record>` now has regression coverage proving FRX root settings delete flags persist and refreshed report-layout JSON moves root settings to deleted-settings metadata without disturbing section/object metadata.
- Studio report settings restore mutation notes — Studio host `--restore-object --record <settings-record>` now has regression coverage proving deleted FRX root settings rows can be restored and refreshed report-layout JSON returns root settings to live settings metadata without disturbing section/object metadata.
- Studio deleted report settings selection notes — Studio host `--json --record <settings-record>` now has regression coverage proving DBF-deleted root settings rows still classify as report settings selections and expose selected-settings provenance while live/deleted settings counts remain distinct.
- Studio deleted report section selection notes — Studio host `--json --record <section-record>` now has regression coverage proving DBF-deleted section rows still classify as report section selections and expose selected-section metadata while live/deleted section counts remain distinct.
- Studio deleted report object selection notes — Studio host `--json --record <layout-object-record>` now has regression coverage proving DBF-deleted report layout objects still classify as report object selections, expose deleted selected-object metadata, and keep containing-section metadata explicitly unavailable.
- Studio unplaced report object selection notes — Studio host `--json --record <layout-object-record>` now has regression coverage proving live unplaced report layout objects classify as report object selections, expose selected-object metadata, and keep containing-section metadata explicitly unavailable.
- Studio label layout parity notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `reportLayout.isLabel`, selected label object metadata, selected label section metadata, live settings, live sections, and containing-section metadata use the same JSON contract as `.frx` report assets.
- Studio label layout nudge mutation notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--nudge-object` mutates stable-selector label layout object `HPOS`/`VPOS` fields and refreshed label report-layout JSON recomputes object geometry while preserving `isLabel`.
- Studio label layout alignment mutation notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--align-object --alignment-mode left` mutates stable-selector label layout object `HPOS` fields and refreshed label report-layout JSON recomputes horizontal geometry while preserving `isLabel`.
- Studio label layout resize mutation notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--resize-object --resize-mode size` mutates stable-selector label layout object `WIDTH`/`HEIGHT` fields and refreshed label report-layout JSON recomputes span and edge geometry while preserving `isLabel`.
- Studio label layout snap-to-grid mutation notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--snap-object --snap-mode both` mutates stable-selector label layout object `HPOS`/`VPOS` fields to grid-aligned values and refreshed label report-layout JSON recomputes geometry while preserving `isLabel`.
- Studio label layout delete-state mutation notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--delete-object` marks stable-selector label layout objects deleted, moves them into deleted-object metadata, keeps selected deleted-object JSON available, and preserves `isLabel`.
- Studio label layout restore mutation notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--restore-object` clears stable-selector label layout object delete flags, restores live section membership and selected containing-section metadata, and preserves `isLabel`.
- Studio label layout distribution mutation notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--distribute-object --distribution-mode horizontal` redistributes stable-selector label layout object `HPOS` values, preserves vertical geometry and size, refreshes section order/count metadata, and preserves `isLabel`.
- Studio label layout reorder mutation notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--reorder-object --placement before` persists stable-selector physical object order changes, refreshes section object index/count metadata, and preserves `isLabel`.
- Studio label layout duplicate mutation notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--duplicate-object --new-unique-id` appends a copied label layout object with the requested replacement `UNIQUEID`, refreshes live section membership/index/count metadata, and preserves `isLabel`.
- Studio label layout rename identity mutation notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--rename-object --new-unique-id` replaces stable-selector label layout object `UNIQUEID` values while preserving object count/order, refreshing live section membership metadata, and preserving `isLabel`.
- Studio label section delete-state mutation notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--delete-object --record <section-record>` persists section delete flags, moves label sections into deleted-section metadata, preserves selected deleted-section JSON, leaves former section objects unplaced without fabricated containing-section metadata, and preserves `isLabel`.
- Studio label section restore mutation notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--restore-object --record <section-record>` clears section delete flags, restores live section counts, clears deleted-section counts, returns former unplaced objects to section membership, restores containing-section metadata, and preserves `isLabel`.
- Studio label settings delete-state mutation notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--delete-object --record <settings-record>` persists root/settings delete flags, moves root settings into deleted-setting metadata, preserves selected deleted-settings JSON, preserves section/object metadata, and preserves `isLabel`.
- Studio label settings restore mutation notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--restore-object --record <settings-record>` clears root/settings delete flags, restores live settings counts, clears deleted-setting counts, preserves selected settings JSON, preserves section/object metadata, and preserves `isLabel`.
- Studio selected label settings JSON notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <settings-record>` exposes shared `selectedReportSettings` JSON, selected settings kind/availability, memo-line and direct setting provenance, section metadata, layout-object metadata, and `isLabel`.
- Studio selected deleted label settings JSON notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <deleted-settings-record>` exposes shared `selectedReportSettings` JSON, selected settings kind/availability, deleted-setting counts/provenance, section metadata, deleted-object metadata, and `isLabel`.
- Studio selected deleted label section JSON notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <deleted-section-record>` exposes shared selected-section JSON, selected section kind/availability, deleted-section counts/provenance, unplaced former section objects without fabricated containing-section metadata, and `isLabel`.
- Studio selected deleted label object JSON notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <deleted-object-record>` exposes shared selected-object JSON, selected object kind/availability, deleted-object counts, explicit null selected containing-section metadata, and `isLabel`.
- Studio selected unplaced label object JSON notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <unplaced-object-record>` exposes shared selected-object JSON, selected object kind/availability, unplaced-object counts, explicit null selected containing-section metadata, and `isLabel`.
- Studio unselected label layout summary JSON notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--json` without `--record` exposes shared report-layout summary JSON, label identity, live setting/section counts, deleted/unplaced object counts, setting provenance, and explicit `selectedReportSelectionKind: none` no-selection metadata.
- Studio selected label section no-object JSON notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <section-record>` exposes shared selected-section JSON while keeping selected-object and containing-object-section metadata explicitly unavailable/null.
- Studio selected deleted label section no-object JSON notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <deleted-section-record>` exposes shared deleted selected-section JSON while keeping selected-object and containing-object-section metadata explicitly unavailable/null.
- Studio selected label settings no-section/object JSON notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <settings-record>` exposes shared selected-settings JSON while keeping selected-section, selected-object, and containing-object-section metadata explicitly unavailable/null.
- Studio selected deleted label settings no-section/object JSON notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <deleted-settings-record>` exposes shared deleted selected-settings JSON while keeping selected-section, selected-object, and containing-object-section metadata explicitly unavailable/null.
- Studio selected label object no-section/settings JSON notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <object-record>` exposes shared selected-object JSON and containing-object-section metadata while keeping selected-section and selected-settings metadata explicitly unavailable/null.
- Studio selected deleted label object no-section/settings JSON notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <deleted-object-record>` exposes shared selected deleted-object JSON and null containing-object-section metadata while keeping selected-section and selected-settings metadata explicitly unavailable/null.
- Studio selected unplaced label object no-section/settings JSON notes — Studio host `.lbx` label assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <unplaced-object-record>` exposes shared selected unplaced-object JSON and null containing-object-section metadata while keeping selected-section and selected-settings metadata explicitly unavailable/null.
- Studio selected report object no-section/settings JSON notes — Studio host `.frx` report assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <object-record>` exposes shared selected-object JSON and containing-object-section metadata while keeping selected-section and selected-settings metadata explicitly unavailable/null.
- Studio selected deleted report object no-section/settings JSON notes — Studio host `.frx` report assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <deleted-object-record>` exposes shared selected deleted-object JSON and null containing-object-section metadata while keeping selected-section and selected-settings metadata explicitly unavailable/null.
- Studio selected unplaced report object no-section/settings JSON notes — Studio host `.frx` report assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <unplaced-object-record>` exposes shared selected unplaced-object JSON and null containing-object-section metadata while keeping selected-section and selected-settings metadata explicitly unavailable/null.
- Studio selected report section no-object/settings JSON notes — Studio host `.frx` report assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <section-record>` exposes shared selected-section JSON while keeping selected-object, containing-object-section, and selected-settings metadata explicitly unavailable/null.
- Studio selected deleted report section no-object/settings JSON notes — Studio host `.frx` report assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <deleted-section-record>` exposes shared deleted selected-section JSON while keeping selected-object, containing-object-section, and selected-settings metadata explicitly unavailable/null.
- Studio selected report settings no-section/object JSON notes — Studio host `.frx` report assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <settings-record>` exposes shared selected-settings JSON while keeping selected-section, selected-object, and containing-object-section metadata explicitly unavailable/null.
- Studio selected deleted report settings no-section/object JSON notes — Studio host `.frx` report assets now have regression coverage proving `--json --record <deleted-settings-record>` exposes shared deleted selected-settings JSON while keeping selected-section, selected-object, and containing-object-section metadata explicitly unavailable/null.
- Studio host selection-context override notes — Studio launch requests now accept repeatable `--selection-context` tokens so external hosts can request active visual-object, visual-method, report-expression, project-item, or data-environment context surfaces explicitly instead of relying only on asset-kind defaults.
- Studio host method-symbol inference notes — form/class launches with method-like symbols such as `cmdSave.Click` now infer the visual-method designer context automatically when no explicit selection-context override is present, aligning default context-aware editor output with Visual Studio method navigation.
- Studio launch-selection metadata notes — opened Studio documents and Studio host JSON now preserve the requested selection symbol, line, column, and record index so designer hosts can correlate active context output with the Visual Studio navigation target.
- Studio/Visual Studio launch-contract error notes — `test_studio_host` now covers malformed and missing numeric selection values for VS launch-contract `--record`, `--line`, and `--column` arguments, proving those parser paths reject invalid navigation metadata with explicit errors.
- Studio DataEnvironment symbol inference notes — form/class launches targeting DataEnvironment symbols such as `Dataenvironment.OpenTables` now infer the data-environment designer context before generic visual-method fallback, keeping data-environment actions and builders visible for those navigation targets.
- Studio designer context count notes — designer-context results and Studio host JSON now expose editor-action, builder, and toolbox-item counts derived from the filtered context descriptor vectors.
- Studio visual context builder notes — visual-object designer contexts now expose the VFP-compatible form builder alongside existing control/grid builders, while visual-method contexts keep the narrower control-builder set.
- Studio label context notes — LBX/LBT documents now use an explicit `label_expression` designer context with shared expression editing, report-safe toolbox items, and label-wizard builder metadata instead of inheriting report-builder metadata.
- Studio class context notes — VCX/VCT documents now use an explicit `class_designer` designer context with property-grid, method-editor, class-safe toolbox items, and class-builder metadata instead of inheriting form/control builder metadata from the generic visual-object context.
- Studio menu context notes — MNX/MNT documents now use an explicit `menu_item` designer context with property-grid actions, a dedicated menu command/detail editor action (`edit-menu-command` / `studio.menu_command_editor.open`), and menu-designer builder metadata, while reporting zero toolbox items and excluding unrelated form/report builder metadata.
- Studio container context notes — Studio launch requests can now target an explicit `container_object` designer context that exposes property-grid, method-editor, control/grid builder metadata, and container-safe toolbox items without inheriting top-level form/class/report/menu/project/data-environment builders.
- Studio selected-container inference notes — form/class launches with a selected Container, PageFrame, Page, Grid, or Column record now infer the `container_object` designer context when no explicit selection-context override is present, keeping nested container editing on container-safe builders and toolbox entries.
- Studio report/label DataEnvironment inference notes — DataEnvironment symbols and selected DataEnvironment records now infer the `data_environment` designer context for report and label assets as well as form/class assets, while expression-context defaults remain unchanged for ordinary FRX/LBX launches.
- Studio toolbox descriptor creation notes — Studio now resolves toolbox palette descriptors into rollback-safe VFP visual-object create requests through `create_visual_object_from_toolbox_item`, including default object-name generation, descriptor class/baseclass mapping, optional identity/parent metadata, caller-provided field values, and no-mutation failure for unknown toolbox item ids.
- Studio selection-context toolbox creation execution notes — Studio now resolves selected-context single and ordered batch toolbox creates through `create_visual_object_from_toolbox_selection` and `create_visual_objects_from_toolbox_selection`, preserving launch metadata, generated-name reservation, explicit per-item identity/parent/field values, record indexes, created-object metadata, and no-mutation failure behavior for unsupported selections or context-incompatible toolbox items.
- Studio toolbox dispatch-sourced creation execution notes — Studio now executes admitted toolbox palette dispatch single-item and ordered batch creates through `create_visual_object_from_toolbox_dispatch` and `create_visual_objects_from_toolbox_dispatch`, preserving dispatch context, selected-parent defaults, generated or explicit identity metadata, lower-level create-result metadata, and no-mutation failure behavior for non-admitted, unavailable, stale, duplicate, or lower-layer invalid requests.
- Studio host dispatch-sourced creation execution JSON notes — `copperfin_studio_host` now exposes `--toolbox-create-from-dispatch` and `--toolbox-create-batch-from-dispatch`, routing admitted toolbox palette dispatches into single-object or ordered batch toolbox creation while emitting dispatch-sourced create-plan or batch-plan metadata, lower-level create-result metadata, created-object metadata, dry-run state, mutation state, parser errors, and clean no-stale-metadata failure output.
- Studio host toolbox-create JSON notes — `copperfin_studio_host` now exposes toolbox descriptor creation through `--toolbox-create`, `--selection-toolbox-create`, `--toolbox-create-batch`, and `--selection-toolbox-create-batch` plus optional identity, parent, and repeatable field-value arguments, returning deterministic JSON result metadata for external designer shells.
- Studio toolbox creation context-filter notes — toolbox descriptor creation now accepts an optional toolbox context and rejects context-incompatible controls, such as form-only TextBox creation in report contexts, without mutating the asset; the Studio host exposes this through `--toolbox-context`.
- Studio toolbox creation planning notes — Studio now exposes non-mutating single and batch toolbox creation planners that validate descriptor metadata, toolbox context compatibility, generated or explicit object names, identity collisions, parent metadata, and caller field values before any asset write.
- Studio toolbox create-from-dispatch planning notes — Studio now composes admitted toolbox palette dispatches with non-mutating single and ordered batch creation planners, preserving dispatch context, selected-parent defaults, generated-name reservation, per-item overrides, and no-mutation failure behavior before any asset write.
- Studio host toolbox creation planning JSON notes — `copperfin_studio_host` now exposes `--toolbox-create-plan`, `--selection-toolbox-create-plan`, `--toolbox-create-batch-plan`, `--selection-toolbox-create-batch-plan`, `--toolbox-create-from-dispatch-plan`, `--toolbox-create-batch-from-dispatch-plan`, `--toolbox-create-plan-catalog`, `--selection-toolbox-create-plan-catalog`, `--toolbox-create-batch-plan-catalog`, and `--selection-toolbox-create-batch-plan-catalog` so external designer shells can preview single-item, selected-context single-item, ordered batch, selected-context ordered batch, dispatch-sourced single-item, dispatch-sourced batch, per-context single-item catalog, selected-context single-item catalog, per-context batch catalog, and selected-context batch catalog creates without mutating visual assets; execution host JSON now includes `--toolbox-create-from-dispatch` and `--toolbox-create-batch-from-dispatch` for admitted dispatch-sourced creation.
- Studio toolbox creation dispatch planning notes — Studio now exposes non-executing dispatch planners for single, batch, dispatch-sourced single-item, dispatch-sourced ordered batch, single-item catalog, selected-context single-item catalog, context-wide batch catalog, and selected-context batch catalog toolbox creates, with explicit create-operation admission required before dispatch arguments claim mutation intent.
- Studio host toolbox creation dispatch JSON notes — `copperfin_studio_host` now exposes `--toolbox-create-dispatch-plan`, `--selection-toolbox-create-dispatch-plan`, `--toolbox-create-dispatch-from-dispatch-plan`, `--toolbox-create-batch-dispatch-plan`, `--toolbox-create-batch-dispatch-from-dispatch-plan`, `--toolbox-create-dispatch-catalog`, `--selection-toolbox-create-dispatch-catalog`, `--toolbox-create-batch-dispatch-catalog`, and `--selection-toolbox-create-batch-dispatch-catalog`, emitting deterministic create-command argument contracts while keeping `executed` false and leaving visual assets unchanged.
- Studio host stable-selector property edit notes — `copperfin_studio_host --set-property` now accepts `--object-name` and `--unique-id` selectors, so external designer hosts can edit selected controls by stable VFP object identity instead of depending only on DBF record indexes.
- Studio host clear-property command notes — `copperfin_studio_host --clear-property` now exposes rollback-safe visual property clearing by record index, object name, or unique id, with ambiguous set/clear requests rejected before mutation.
- Studio host rename-property command notes — `copperfin_studio_host --rename-property` now exposes rollback-safe memo-backed visual property renaming by record index, object name, or unique id, with `--new-property-name` validation and ambiguous property command rejection before mutation.
- Studio host delete-object command notes — `copperfin_studio_host --delete-object` now exposes rollback-safe selected-object deletion by record index, object name, or unique id, with delete/property command ambiguity rejected before mutation.
- Studio host restore-object command notes — `copperfin_studio_host --restore-object` now exposes rollback-safe selected-object restoration by record index, object name, or unique id, with delete/restore/property command ambiguity rejected before mutation.
- Studio host duplicate-object command notes — `copperfin_studio_host --duplicate-object` now exposes rollback-safe selected-object duplication by record index, object name, or unique id, accepts replacement object/name/unique-id fields, and rejects duplicate/delete/restore/property command ambiguity before mutation.
- Studio host rename-object command notes — `copperfin_studio_host --rename-object` now exposes rollback-safe selected-object identity renaming by record index, object name, or unique id, accepts replacement object/name/unique-id fields, and rejects empty identity plus rename-object/delete/restore/duplicate/property command ambiguity before mutation.
- Studio host reparent-object command notes — `copperfin_studio_host --reparent-object` now exposes rollback-safe selected-object reparenting by record index, object name, or unique id, accepts parent object-name/unique-id selectors plus `--clear-parent`, and rejects missing parent selectors plus reparent-object/delete/restore/duplicate/rename-object/property command ambiguity before mutation.
- Studio host reorder-object command notes — `copperfin_studio_host --reorder-object` now exposes rollback-safe selected-object reordering by record index, object name, or unique id, accepts front/back/before/after placement with relative target object-name/unique-id selectors, and rejects missing placement plus reorder-object/delete/restore/duplicate/rename-object/reparent-object/property command ambiguity before mutation.
- Studio host group-object command notes — `copperfin_studio_host --group-object` now exposes rollback-safe selected-child grouping through repeated `--group-child-object-name` / `--group-child-unique-id` selectors and repeated `--field-value name=value` group-container assignments, and rejects group-object/delete/restore/duplicate/rename-object/reparent-object/reorder-object/ungroup-object/property command ambiguity before mutation.
- Studio host align-object command notes — `copperfin_studio_host --align-object` now exposes rollback-safe selected-object alignment through stable `--anchor-object-name` / `--anchor-unique-id` selectors, repeated `--align-target-object-name` / `--align-target-unique-id` selectors, and `--alignment-mode left|right|top|bottom|horizontal-center|vertical-center`, and rejects align-object/delete/restore/duplicate/rename-object/reparent-object/reorder-object/group-object/ungroup-object/property command ambiguity before mutation.
- Studio host resize-object command notes — `copperfin_studio_host --resize-object` now exposes rollback-safe selected-object resize-to-anchor behavior through stable `--anchor-object-name` / `--anchor-unique-id` selectors, repeated `--resize-target-object-name` / `--resize-target-unique-id` selectors, and `--resize-mode width|height|size`, and rejects resize-object/delete/restore/duplicate/rename-object/reparent-object/reorder-object/group-object/align-object/ungroup-object/property command ambiguity before mutation.
- Studio host distribute-object command notes — `copperfin_studio_host --distribute-object` now exposes rollback-safe selected-object distribution through repeated `--distribute-target-object-name` / `--distribute-target-unique-id` selectors and `--distribution-mode horizontal|vertical`, and rejects distribute-object/delete/restore/duplicate/rename-object/reparent-object/reorder-object/group-object/align-object/resize-object/ungroup-object/property command ambiguity before mutation.
- Studio host snap-object command notes — `copperfin_studio_host --snap-object` now exposes rollback-safe selected-object snap-to-grid behavior through repeated `--snap-target-object-name` / `--snap-target-unique-id` selectors, `--snap-mode horizontal|vertical|both`, `--grid-width`, and `--grid-height`, and rejects snap-object/delete/restore/duplicate/rename-object/reparent-object/reorder-object/group-object/align-object/resize-object/distribute-object/ungroup-object/property command ambiguity before mutation.
- Studio host nudge-object command notes — `copperfin_studio_host --nudge-object` now exposes rollback-safe selected-object nudging through repeated `--nudge-target-object-name` / `--nudge-target-unique-id` selectors, `--nudge-mode horizontal|vertical|both`, `--delta-hpos`, and `--delta-vpos`, and rejects nudge-object/delete/restore/duplicate/rename-object/reparent-object/reorder-object/group-object/align-object/resize-object/distribute-object/snap-object/ungroup-object/property command ambiguity before mutation.
- Studio host tab-order-object command notes — `copperfin_studio_host --tab-order-object` now exposes rollback-safe selected-object TABINDEX sequencing through repeated `--tab-order-target-object-name` / `--tab-order-target-unique-id` selectors and optional `--starting-tab-index`, and rejects tab-order-object/delete/restore/duplicate/rename-object/reparent-object/reorder-object/group-object/align-object/resize-object/distribute-object/snap-object/nudge-object/ungroup-object/property command ambiguity before mutation.
