- Studio host ungroup-object command notes — `copperfin_studio_host --ungroup-object` now exposes rollback-safe selected-container ungrouping by record index, object name, or unique id, and rejects ungroup-object/delete/restore/duplicate/rename-object/reparent-object/reorder-object/property command ambiguity before mutation.
- Visual object undo affected-count notes — visual property undo now surfaces an affected-object count through generic edit results, with missing-history failures reporting zero affected objects.
- Visual object base property-edit affected-count notes — base single and multi-property visual object edit APIs now surface affected-object counts through generic edit results, with failed requests reporting zero affected objects.
- Visual object structural-command affected-count notes — visual object reparent, rename, reorder, deleted-state, and subtree deleted-state commands now surface affected-object counts through generic edit results for designer tree operations, with failed requests reporting zero affected objects.
- Visual object property-command affected-count notes — visual property clear, copy, move, rename, and reorder commands now surface affected-object counts through generic edit results for single and batch property-grid operations, with failed requests reporting zero affected objects.
- Visual object property-grid search notes — `filter_visual_object_properties` now provides non-mutating selected-object property-grid filtering over existing property-list results, with case-insensitive matching across direct and memo-backed property names, values, backing metadata, and source-line metadata while preserving selector resolution and no-undo behavior.
- Visual object method-command affected-count notes — visual method update, delete, rename, copy, move, and reorder commands now surface affected-object counts through generic edit results for single and batch code-pane operations, with failed requests reporting zero affected objects.
- Visual object helper-backed property affected-count notes — shared logical and non-negative numeric designer property command coverage now proves generic edit-result affected-object counts, with failed empty-selection requests reporting zero affected objects.
- Visual object text-property affected-count notes — additional text designer property commands now surface affected-object counts through generic edit results, with failed text property requests reporting zero affected objects.
- Visual object scalar-property affected-count notes — representative logical and text scalar designer property commands now surface affected-object counts through generic edit results, with failed scalar property requests reporting zero affected objects.
- Visual object tab-command affected-count notes — tab-order and tab-stop designer commands now surface affected-object counts through generic edit results, with failed tab command requests reporting zero affected objects.
- Visual object geometry-command affected-count notes — alignment, resize, distribution, snap-to-grid, and nudge operations now surface affected-object counts through generic edit results, with failed geometry commands reporting zero affected objects.
- Visual object batch-edit affected-count notes — rollback-safe batch visual object edits now report the number of edited objects through generic edit results, while failed or empty batch edit requests report zero affected objects.
- Visual object ungroup-result container identity notes — ungrouping now reports the selected container's resolved object name, unique id, and parent name alongside target-parent and child-count metadata, while failed ungroup requests return empty container identity metadata.
- Visual object group-result container identity notes — grouping now reports the created container's resolved object name, unique id, and parent name while preserving grouped-child-count reporting, and failed grouping requests return empty container identity metadata.
- Visual object subtree-duplicate-result root identity notes — selected-object subtree duplication now reports the appended copied root's resolved object name, unique id, and parent name while preserving copied-count reporting, and failed subtree-duplicate requests return empty root identity metadata.
- Visual object batch-duplicate-result identity notes — batch selected-object duplication now reports each appended duplicate's record index, resolved object name, unique id, and parent name in append order, while failed batch-duplicate requests return empty identity metadata.
- Visual object duplicate-result identity notes — selected-object duplication now reports the appended duplicate's resolved object name, unique id, and parent name directly from the duplicated visual asset record, while failed duplicate requests return empty identity metadata.
- Visual object batch-create-result identity notes — batch toolbox create operations now report each appended object's record index, resolved object name, unique id, and parent name in append order, while failed batch-create requests return empty identity metadata.
- Visual object create-result identity notes — toolbox create operations now report the appended object's resolved object name, unique id, and parent name directly from the created visual asset record, while failed create requests return empty identity metadata.
- Studio selected-record DataEnvironment inference notes — form/class launches with `--record` targeting parsed DataEnvironment object metadata now infer the data-environment designer context when no explicit override is present, while non-DataEnvironment selected records keep visual-object defaults.
- Studio selected-object JSON notes — Studio host JSON now emits a compact `selectedObject` summary for explicit record selections that match parsed designer objects, including direct selected-object property snapshots with stable `name`, `type`, `isNull`, and `value` fields, giving external hosts direct access to selected record/object identity and property-grid data without scanning the full object array.
- Studio selected-object raw-code fallback notes — Studio host JSON now has regression coverage proving malformed, oversized, and dot-leading visual object `OBJTYPE`/`OBJCODE` source values keep document inspection non-failing, default parsed `objectTypeCode`/`objectCode` to zero, and remain available as raw selected-object and object-list property metadata for designer diagnostics.
- Studio object-list JSON notes — Studio host JSON now emits parsed designer-object identity fields directly on each `document.objects[]` entry, so object browsers can consume `objectName`, `uniqueId`, `parentName`, `className`, and `baseclassName` without reparsing the property list.
- Studio object hierarchy JSON notes — Studio object snapshots now derive direct child counts from parsed object-name/parent-name relationships, and Studio host JSON emits `childCount` for selected-object summaries and full object-list entries so designer tree panes can show hierarchy affordances without rescanning all objects.
- Studio object parent-link JSON notes — Studio object snapshots now resolve nullable parent record links from parsed object-name/parent-name relationships, and Studio host JSON emits `parentRecordIndex` for selected-object summaries and full object-list entries so designer tree panes can attach child nodes without resolving parent names themselves.
- Studio object-path JSON notes — Studio object snapshots now derive dot-separated object paths from parsed object names and resolved parent record links, and Studio host JSON emits `objectPath` for selected-object summaries and full object-list entries so designer tree/search panes can identify nested objects without rebuilding parent chains.
- Studio object-code metadata JSON notes — Studio host JSON now emits raw parsed object code metadata (`objectTypeCode`, `objectCode`, and `platform`) for selected-object summaries and full object-list entries so designer panes can distinguish object families without scanning DBF property rows.
- Studio selected-object deletion-state JSON notes — Studio host JSON now emits the parsed `deleted` boolean directly on non-null selected-object summaries, matching the full object-list contract so hosts can render selected deleted records without consulting `document.objects[]`.
- Studio property provenance JSON notes — Studio host JSON now emits property provenance (`fieldIndex`, `memoBlockNumber`, `derivedFromPropertyBlob`, and nullable `sourceLineIndex`) for selected-object and full object-list properties so property grids can locate the backing DBF field or serialized property source without reparsing asset records.
- Studio object property-count JSON notes — Studio host JSON now emits direct `propertyCount` values for selected-object summaries and full object-list entries so designer/property-grid panes can size or lazy-load rows without scanning the emitted property arrays first.
- Studio document object-count JSON notes — Studio host JSON now emits document-level `objectCount` and `deletedObjectCount` values derived from the emitted object snapshot vector so designer panes can size object browsers without scanning `document.objects[]` first.
- Studio document root-object-count JSON notes — Studio host JSON now emits document-level `rootObjectCount` from parsed object snapshots with no resolved parent record link so designer tree panes can size top-level nodes without scanning `document.objects[]` first.
- Studio selected-object availability JSON notes — Studio host JSON now emits document-level `selectedObjectAvailable`, set only when an explicit selected record resolves to a parsed object snapshot, so hosts can distinguish unmatched selected records from real selected objects without relying only on `selectedObject: null`.
- Studio object child-record-link JSON notes — Studio object snapshots now carry direct child record-index arrays, and Studio host JSON emits `childRecordIndexes` for selected-object summaries and full object-list entries so designer tree panes can expand nodes without scanning all objects for matching parent links.
- Studio document root-record-link JSON notes — Studio host JSON now emits document-level `rootRecordIndexes` from parsed object snapshots with no resolved parent record link so designer tree panes can seed top-level nodes without scanning `document.objects[]` first.
- Studio document leaf-record-link JSON notes — Studio host JSON now emits document-level `leafObjectCount` and `leafRecordIndexes` from parsed object snapshots with no child record links so designer tree panes can identify terminal nodes without scanning `document.objects[]` first.
- Studio object tree-depth JSON notes — Studio object snapshots now derive hierarchy depth from resolved parent record links, and Studio host JSON emits per-object `objectDepth` plus document-level `maxObjectDepth` so designer tree panes can indent and virtualize hierarchy rows without rebuilding parent chains.
- Studio object sibling-order JSON notes — Studio object snapshots now derive sibling index/count metadata from resolved parent record links, and Studio host JSON emits per-object `siblingIndex` and `siblingCount` so designer tree panes can navigate stable object ordering without regrouping client-side.
- Studio object ancestor-link JSON notes — Studio object snapshots now derive root-to-parent ancestor record links from resolved parent chains, and Studio host JSON emits per-object `ancestorRecordIndexes` so designer tree panes and context-aware editors can expand parent chains without reparsing paths or walking links client-side.
- Visual editor outline hierarchy notes — `VisualObjectSnapshot` now exposes resolved parent-record availability/index plus direct child counts across object, child, descendant, and ancestor listing APIs so designer object browsers can render hierarchy affordances without issuing extra per-row hierarchy calls.
- Visual editor outline path/order notes — `VisualObjectSnapshot` now exposes object path, hierarchy depth, ancestor record links, sibling index, and sibling count across object, child, descendant, and ancestor listing APIs so designer object browsers can render stable tree rows from one listing call.
- Visual editor outline count notes — `VisualObjectSnapshot` now exposes property and method counts, derived through the existing property and method parsers, so property grids and method browsers can size affordances from outline rows before loading per-object detail panes.
- Visual object grouping result notes — `VisualObjectGroupResult` now reports the number of selected objects reparented into the new container, while failed grouping paths continue to return zero and preserve rollback behavior.
- Visual object ungrouping result notes — `VisualObjectUngroupResult` now reports the target parent name and resolved parent-record metadata after successful ungroup operations, while root-level and failed ungroup paths report empty/unavailable parent metadata.
- Native-wrapper bridge-request descriptor metadata notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper request JSON now serializes routine kind, source provenance, parameter declaration style, parameter-name list, and schema version beside the existing export name, arity, request media type, and parameter value payloads, so runtime-host bridge requests carry the same descriptor metadata already present in wrapper argv/env scaffolding
- Native-wrapper exported-argument consumption notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now consume DLL integer parameters and FLL `ParamBlk* parm` through `copperfin_build_runtime_bridge_call(...)` parameter payloads without emitting false `(void)` unused markers for those exported arguments
- Native-wrapper request-write execution call-site notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now execute `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_write_request(request_write_plan)` immediately after constructing the request-write plan and pass the execution result into response-read planning, so request documents are materialized through the shared helper while failed request writes force deterministic empty-response fallback behavior
- Native-wrapper request-write execution consumption notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now consume `request_write_execution` through `copperfin_build_runtime_bridge_response_read_plan(...)`; the response-read plan now carries `request_write_succeeded` and returns the shared empty response document when request writing fails
- Native-wrapper response-read execution call-site notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now execute `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_read_response(response_read_plan)` immediately after constructing the response-read plan and pass the resulting response document into `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeResponseArtifact` instead of seeding response artifacts from the empty-response fallback
- Native-wrapper dispatch-execution consumption notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now consume `dispatch_execution` through `copperfin_runtime_bridge_launch_process(...)` without emitting a false `(void)dispatch_execution` unused marker
- Native-wrapper process-launch consumption notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now consume `process_launch` through `copperfin_runtime_bridge_evaluate_host_failure(...)` without emitting a false `(void)process_launch` unused marker
- Native-wrapper host-failure evaluation-consumption notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now consume `host_failure` through `copperfin_runtime_bridge_evaluate_missing_response(...)` without emitting a false `(void)host_failure` unused marker
- Native-wrapper missing-response evaluation-consumption notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now consume `missing_response` through `copperfin_runtime_bridge_evaluate_response_validation(...)` without emitting a false `(void)missing_response` unused marker
- Native-wrapper response-validation evaluation-consumption notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now consume `response_validation_evaluation` through `copperfin_runtime_bridge_admit_response_parse(...)` without emitting a false `(void)response_validation_evaluation` unused marker
- Native-wrapper response-parse execution call-site notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now execute `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_parse_response(response_parse_plan)` after response-parse admission and pass the parsed response into `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeInterpretedResultPlan` so parsed status, return-value, and diagnostics values flow forward through interpretation instead of parse execution staying hidden inside the interpreted-result helper
- Native-wrapper response-parse admission-consumption notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now consume `response_parse_admission` through `copperfin_runtime_bridge_admit_interpreted_result(...)` without emitting a false `(void)response_parse_admission` unused marker
- Native-wrapper interpreted-result execution call-site notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now execute `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_interpreted_result(interpreted_result_plan)` after interpreted-result admission and pass the interpreted success/fallback result into `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeNativeReturnPlan` so native-return planning consumes explicit interpretation output instead of invoking interpretation internally
- Native-wrapper interpreted-result admission-consumption notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now consume `interpreted_result_admission` through `copperfin_runtime_bridge_admit_native_return(...)` without emitting a false `(void)interpreted_result_admission` unused marker
- Native-wrapper native-return execution call-site notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now execute `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_native_return(native_return_plan)` after native-return admission and pass the selected native return metadata into `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeOutcomeSelectionPlan` so outcome selection consumes explicit native-return output instead of invoking native-return execution internally
- Native-wrapper native-return admission-consumption notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now consume `native_return_admission` through `copperfin_runtime_bridge_admit_outcome_selection(...)` without emitting a false `(void)native_return_admission` unused marker
- Native-wrapper outcome-selection execution call-site notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now execute `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_outcome_selection(outcome_selection_plan)` after outcome-selection admission and pass the selected condition/diagnostics/native return metadata into `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeReturnMaterializationPlan` so return materialization consumes explicit outcome-selection output instead of invoking outcome-selection execution internally
- Native-wrapper outcome-selection admission-consumption notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now consume `outcome_selection_admission` through `copperfin_runtime_bridge_admit_return_materialization(...)` without emitting a false `(void)outcome_selection_admission` unused marker
- Native-wrapper return-materialization execution call-site notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now execute `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_return_materialization(return_materialization_plan)` after return-materialization admission and pass the materialized return branch metadata into `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeReturnEmissionPlan` so return emission consumes explicit materialized-return output instead of invoking return-materialization execution internally
- Native-wrapper return-materialization admission-consumption notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now consume `return_materialization_admission` through `copperfin_runtime_bridge_admit_return_emission(...)` without emitting a false `(void)return_materialization_admission` unused marker
- Native-wrapper return-emission execution call-site notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now execute `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_return_emission(return_emission_plan)` after return-emission admission and pass the emitted return block metadata into `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeFinalReturnAdoptionPlan` so final-return adoption consumes explicit return-emission output instead of invoking return-emission execution internally
- Native-wrapper return-emission admission-consumption notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now consume `return_emission_admission` through `copperfin_runtime_bridge_admit_final_return_adoption(...)` without emitting a false `(void)return_emission_admission` unused marker
- Native-wrapper final-return-adoption execution call-site notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now execute `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_final_return_adoption(final_return_adoption_plan)` after final-return-adoption admission and pass the adopted return metadata into `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeReturnActivationPlan` so return activation consumes explicit final-return-adoption output instead of invoking final-return-adoption execution internally
- Native-wrapper final-return-adoption admission-consumption notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now consume `final_return_adoption_admission` through `copperfin_runtime_bridge_admit_return_activation(...)` without emitting a false `(void)final_return_adoption_admission` unused marker
- Native-wrapper return-activation execution call-site notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now execute `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_return_activation(return_activation_plan)` after return-activation admission and pass the activated return metadata into `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeStubReturnPlan` so stub-return handling consumes explicit return-activation output instead of invoking return-activation execution internally
- Native-wrapper return-activation admission-consumption notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now consume `return_activation_admission` through `copperfin_runtime_bridge_admit_stub_return(...)` without emitting a false `(void)return_activation_admission` unused marker
- Native-wrapper stub-return execution call-site notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now execute `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_stub_return(stub_return_plan)` after stub-return admission and pass the routed stub-return metadata into `CopperfinRuntimeBridgePlaceholderReturnValuePlan` so placeholder-return-value planning consumes explicit stub-return output instead of invoking stub-return execution internally
- Native-wrapper stub-return admission-consumption notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now consume `stub_return_admission` through `copperfin_runtime_bridge_admit_placeholder_return_value(...)` without emitting a false `(void)stub_return_admission` unused marker
- Native-wrapper placeholder-return-value execution call-site notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now execute `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_placeholder_return_value(placeholder_return_value_plan)` after placeholder-return-value admission and pass the placeholder return metadata into shared stub-emission execution so stub emission consumes explicit placeholder-return-value output instead of invoking placeholder-return-value execution internally
- Native-wrapper placeholder-return-value admission-consumption notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now consume `placeholder_return_value_admission` through `copperfin_runtime_bridge_admit_placeholder_return_int(...)` without emitting a false `(void)placeholder_return_value_admission` unused marker
- Native-wrapper placeholder-return-int admission-driven execution notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now execute `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_placeholder_return_int(placeholder_return_int_admission)` after placeholder-return-int admission and pass the explicit integer into stub emission, so placeholder-return-int output consumes the admitted int surface instead of re-reading placeholder-return-value metadata
- Native-wrapper stub-emission admission-to-execution notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now pass the explicit placeholder-return integer into `copperfin_runtime_bridge_admit_stub_emission(placeholder_return_int_admission, placeholder_return_int)` and execute `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_stub_emission(stub_emission_admission)` from that admitted surface, so stub-emission execution consumes admission output instead of discarding it
- Native-wrapper stub-emission execution call-site notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now execute `copperfin_runtime_bridge_execute_stub_emission(stub_emission_admission)` after stub-emission admission and pass the emitted stub metadata into return-surface construction so stub-emission return handling consumes explicit stub-emission output instead of invoking stub-emission execution internally
- Native-wrapper stub-emission return-surface call-site notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now build `copperfin_runtime_bridge_build_stub_emission_return_surface(...)` after stub-emission execution and pass the explicit return-surface metadata into direct output application so final output handling consumes explicit return-surface output instead of constructing it internally
- Native-wrapper stub-emission output-application call-site notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper stubs now call `copperfin_runtime_bridge_apply_stub_emission_output(stub_emission_return_surface, ...)` after return-surface construction with the planned return adapter, so final DLL/FLL return application is visible at the generated wrapper call-site instead of hidden inside a shared stub-return emitter
- Native-wrapper descriptor wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeDescriptor`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the descriptor and then build invocations straight from the enriched descriptor instead of passing the wrapper separately at the invocation seam
- Native-wrapper invocation wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeInvocation`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the invocation and then build calls straight from the enriched invocation instead of passing the wrapper separately at the call seam
- Native-wrapper call wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeCall`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the call and then build results straight from the enriched call instead of passing the wrapper separately at the result seam
- Native-wrapper result wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeResult`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the result and then build launch plans straight from the enriched result instead of passing the wrapper separately at the launch seam
- Native-wrapper launch wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeLaunchPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the launch plan and then build observation plans straight from the enriched launch plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the observation seam
- Native-wrapper observation wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeObservationPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the observation plan and then build execution plans straight from the enriched observation plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the execution seam
- Native-wrapper execution wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeExecutionPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the execution plan and then build transport plans straight from the enriched execution plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the transport seam
- Native-wrapper transport wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeTransportPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the transport plan and then build serialization plans straight from the enriched transport plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the serialization seam
- Native-wrapper serialization wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeSerializationPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the serialization plan and then build dispatch plans straight from the enriched serialization plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the dispatch seam
- Native-wrapper dispatch wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeDispatchPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the dispatch plan and then build payload plans straight from the enriched dispatch plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the payload seam
- Native-wrapper payload wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgePayloadPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the payload plan and then build interpretation plans straight from the enriched payload plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the interpretation seam
- Native-wrapper interpretation wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeInterpretationPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the interpretation plan and then build failure-policy plans straight from the enriched interpretation plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the failure-policy seam
- Native-wrapper failure-policy wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeFailurePolicyPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the failure-policy plan and then build response-validation plans straight from the enriched failure-policy plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the response-validation seam
- Native-wrapper response-validation wrapper-plan notes — generated DLL/FLL native-wrapper scaffolds now also carry the stub-emission wrapper contract through `CopperfinRuntimeBridgeResponseValidationPlan`, so exported DLL/FLL stubs now build that wrapper once before constructing the response-validation plan and then build request artifacts straight from the enriched response-validation plan instead of passing the wrapper separately at the request-artifact seam
- Runtime-host AI/MCP policy notes — AI-assisted federation planning now requires the native `ai.mcp` role permission through `COPPERFIN_SECURITY_ROLE` (defaulting to `developer`), so optional AI planner requests do not bypass the existing security profile
- FLL registration notes — generated `FoxInfo` metadata now also carries pipe-separated parameter-name metadata (`tcMode`, `tnLeft|tnRight`, and so on) beside routine kind, source provenance, and arity in the honest wrapper scaffold
- Exported routine metadata notes — DLL/FLL callable contracts now also preserve whether each exported routine used `PARAMETERS` or `LPARAMETERS`, and the FLL `FoxInfo` scaffold carries that declaration-style metadata too
- FLL broad-manifest notes — the runtime/debug manifests now mirror the existing FLL per-function callable metadata, and the debug manifest now also records the generated `.def` / `.fll.api` artifact - `DO`, `DO FORM`, `REPORT FORM`, `LABEL FORM` — command-target coverage now also includes true second-hop macro-expanded routine dispatch such as `DO &cProcDeepHolder`, focused call-binding coverage now also includes true second-hop macro-expanded `WITH` arguments such as `DO testparam WITH &cParamExprDeepHolder`, shared assignment-target coverage now includes true second-hop `&target = value` forms such as `&cTargetDeepHolder = 'MACROASSIGN'`, and focused expression coverage now also includes true second-hop macro-expanded array subscripts and function arguments such as `aData[&cRowExprDeepHolder]` and `LEN(&cFieldExprDeepHolder)`
- `CALL` — first-pass command-path parity now includes `CALL <target> WITH ...` argument binding and external `.prg` target resolution when local routines are not found, including BYREF `@var` write-back through existing call-reference plumbing
- `ACTIVATE POPUP`, `ACTIVATE MENU`, `RELEASE POPUP`, `RELEASE MENU`
- `RETURN`
- `IF/ELSE/ENDIF`
- `DO CASE/CASE/OTHERWISE/ENDCASE`
- `FOR/ENDFOR`
- `DO WHILE/ENDDO`
- `WITH/ENDWITH`
- `TRY/CATCH/FINALLY/ENDTRY`

- `SPAWN`, `AWAIT`, `YIELD`, `ENTER CRITICAL`, `EXIT CRITICAL` — first-pass task/coordination surface with spawned worker completion, cooperative yield, and named in-memory critical sections; the current critical-section policy is now centralized in `docs/25-engine-concurrency-policy.md`, explicitly requires ascending normalized section-name order for nested acquisitions, and rejects blocking wait surfaces while a critical section is held (`AWAIT`, positive-duration `SLEEP`, and lock-retry/backoff under contention, including explicit contested `RLOCK()`), emitting deterministic runtime diagnostics instead of deadlock-prone hangs
- `TEXT/ENDTEXT` with first-pass literal `TEXT TO ... [ADDITIVE] [NOSHOW]` capture, including `TEXT TO ... TEXTMERGE` interpolation coverage for nested `EVAL()` and `&macro` expressions inside merged `<<...>>` segments, including true second-hop command-path forms such as `<<EVAL(&cNameExprDeepHolder)>>` and `<<&cFieldExprDeepHolder>>`, plus custom-delimiter `TEXTMERGE()` recursion over stored expression text, macro-expanded delimiter arguments, and macro-expanded source-template text, including true second-hop source-template forms such as `TEXTMERGE(&cTemplateDeepHolder)` and true second-hop delimiter forms such as `TEXTMERGE(..., &cLeftDelimDeepHolder, &cRightDelimDeepHolder)`; first-pass `EXECSCRIPT()` return-expression coverage now includes direct `RETURN &macro` expansion and macro-expanded script-source text over current runtime state, including true second-hop source-text forms such as `EXECSCRIPT(&cExecScriptDeepHolder)` and true second-hop return-expression forms such as `EXECSCRIPT('RETURN &cExecReturnDeepHolder')`; the combined nested-surface harness now also includes second-hop holder flows across `EVAL()` and `EXECSCRIPT()` inputs; shared `EVAL()` / `EVALUATE()` runtime-state coverage now includes macro-expanded expression text such as `ALIAS()` and `SET('PATH')` plus nested macro-indirection through holder variables, including second-hop forms such as `EVAL(&cEvalExprDeepHolder)`, `EVALUATE(&cEvalExprDeepHolder)`, `EVALUATE(&cEvalPathExprDeepHolder)`, runtime-state `&macro` field-name resolution such as `&cFieldExprDeepHolder`, and direct true second-hop `&macro` expression indirection such as `&cMacroNameDeepHolder`; `LOOKUP()` helper coverage now includes nested macro-indirection for return/search expression text plus alias/tag designators on local and SQL cursors, including second-hop expression forms such as `LOOKUP(&cReturnExprDeepHolder, &cSearchExprDeepHolder, ...)`, second-hop numeric return-expression forms such as `LOOKUP(&cAgeExprDeepHolder, &cSearchExpr, ...)`, second-hop local designator forms such as `LOOKUP(..., &cAliasDeepHolder, &cTagDeepHolder)`, second-hop miss-path default typing for numeric `0` and logical `.F.` return expressions, second-hop SQL expression forms such as `LOOKUP(&cReturnExprDeepHolder, &cSearchExprDeepHolder, ...)`, full all-argument second-hop SQL forms such as `LOOKUP(&cReturnExprDeepHolder, &cSearchExprDeepHolder, &cAliasDeepHolder, &cTagDeepHolder)`, and second-hop SQL designator forms such as `LOOKUP(..., &cAliasDeepHolder, &cTagDeepHolder)`; parameter-default coverage now also includes true second-hop macro defaults such as `LPARAMETERS nValue = &cDefaultExprDeepHolder`; macro dot-suffix coverage now includes nested-holder forms before suffix concatenation through second-hop cases such as `&cTypeDeepHolder.Name`, `&cFieldDeepHolder.`, and the literal fallback branch `&cStemDeepHolder.World`; direct `&macro` expression indirection coverage now also includes a true second-hop fallback-text branch via `&cFallbackExprDeepHolder`; embedded macro identifier coverage now includes nested-holder forms such as `m&cType2Holder.ID` and `m&cType2DeepHolder.ID`; and alias-qualified macro field access coverage now includes direct true second-hop alias reads such as `&cAliasDeepHolder..NAME` / `&cAliasDeepHolder..AGE`, the dynamic `AGE` second-hop form `&cAliasDeepHolder..&cAgeFieldHolder`, plus nested-holder alias and field-name variables through forms such as `&cAliasDeepHolder..&cNestedFieldHolder`

- `DELETE`, `RECALL` — current-record commands plus `ALL`, `NEXT <n>`, `RECORD <n>`, and `REST` physical record scopes composed with `FOR`, `WHILE`, `IN`, active filters, and the existing `SET DELETED` behavior for local and synthetic SQL/result cursors
- `DELETE FROM`
- `INSERT INTO`
- `PACK`, `ZAP`
- `SET FILTER TO/OFF` — cursor-scoped filter state for local and synthetic SQL/result cursors, including macro-expanded expression text through the shared `SET` helper and true second-hop holder forms such as `SET FILTER TO &cFilterExprDeepHolder IN sqlcust`; installing or clearing a filter preserves the current physical pointer, immediate field access, `RECNO()`, `BOF()`, `EOF()`, `FOUND()`, selected work area, and targeted non-selected work area until navigation/search evaluates the filter
- `SET FIELDS TO/ON/OFF` — first-pass session-scoped field visibility for runtime field lookup, including list readback through `SET('FIELDS')`; the shipped visibility lane now also honors `LIKE` / `EXCEPT` filters for both direct field resolution and consumers such as `BROWSE`, including true second-hop holder forms such as `SET FIELDS TO &cFieldsSpecDeepHolder`
- `SET ORDER TO TAG`
- `SEEK`
- `GO` / `GOTO`
- `SKIP`
- `USE IN <alias>` alias-target closure now also has focused true second-hop macro proof, including forms such as `USE IN &cAliasExprDeepHolder`
- `BROWSE` — first-pass headless/runtime browse event lane over local/runtime cursors; honors selected or `IN`-targeted cursor context, reuses session `SET FILTER` / `SET FIELDS` state by default, now including `LIKE` / `EXCEPT` field-visibility filters, accepts inline `FIELDS` and `FOR` clause capture, and emits `runtime.browse` metadata for later host/UI wiring
- `EDIT`, `CHANGE`, `INPUT`, `ACCEPT` — first-pass headless interactive command lane that emits runtime events (`runtime.edit`, `runtime.change`, `runtime.input`, `runtime.accept`) with captured prompt/target/field metadata for host-driven UI integration; `EDIT` / `CHANGE` now also surface effective selected-cursor/view metadata when a local cursor is active, including visible fields and active filter state, while `INPUT` / `ACCEPT` now reuse that same selected-cursor/view metadata and assign deterministic empty-string headless results for `TO <target>` variables
- `WAIT`, `KEYBOARD`, `DISPLAY`, `LIST`, `INPUT`, `ACCEPT`, `CALCULATE`, `COUNT`, `SUM`, `AVERAGE`, `TOTAL` — first-pass headless interaction/event lane that emits `runtime.wait`, `runtime.keyboard`, `runtime.display`, `runtime.list`, `runtime.input`, `runtime.accept`, and aggregate runtime events with captured mode/clause payloads for host integration; `WAIT` now captures resolved prompt/timeout/target state plus source expressions for common `WAIT [WINDOW] ... [TIMEOUT ...] [TO <target>] [NOWAIT] [NOCLEAR]` forms and assigns deterministic empty-string headless `TO` results, including true second-hop holder target forms such as `TO &cWaitTargetDeepHolder`; `INPUT` / `ACCEPT` now mirror that resolved prompt/target payload fidelity and deterministic empty-string `TO` behavior, including true second-hop holder target forms such as `TO &cInputTargetDeepHolder`; `KEYBOARD` now captures resolved key payloads plus source expressions and common `PLAIN` / `CLEAR` flags, `DISPLAY` / `LIST` now surface adjacent `STRUCTURE` / `STATUS` / `MEMORY` metadata in addition to the richer `RECORDS` lane, `DISPLAY` / `LIST RECORDS` now also retain `WHILE` clause payload detail and true second-hop `IN` target detail such as `IN &cAliasDeepHolder`, `DISPLAY` / `LIST STRUCTURE` now also has true second-hop `IN` target proof via forms such as `IN &cAliasDeepHolder`, and aggregate command events now also expose targeted cursor/work-area, visible fields, active filter, scope, `FOR` / `WHILE`, output-variable, and raw/resolved `IN` target detail for host/runtime readback, including true second-hop aggregate `TO` target forms such as `TO &cCountDeepHolder`, `TO &cSumDeepHolder`, and `TO &cCalcDeepHolder`
- `GETFILE`, `PUTFILE`, `GETDIR`, `INPUTBOX` — first-pass headless dialog-helper command lane; parser now captures both common clause-keyword forms and parenthesized/positional argument forms for prompt/title/default/filter/target metadata, and dispatcher emits `runtime.getfile`, `runtime.putfile`, `runtime.getdir`, and `runtime.inputbox` events while assigning deterministic empty-string first-pass results to `TO <target>` variables in headless mode
- `PUSH KEY` / `POP KEY`, `PUSH MENU` / `POP MENU`, `PUSH POPUP` / `POP POPUP` — first-pass session-scoped key/menu/popup stack semantics with deterministic push/pop depth behavior and runtime events (`runtime.push_*` / `runtime.pop_*`) including empty-pop signaling
- `SAVE TO`, `RESTORE FROM` — first-pass memvar persistence lane with `.mem` auto-extension behavior, `LIKE`/`EXCEPT` filtering, `ADDITIVE` merge support, robust escaped text serialization, and explicit empty-value markers for stable round-trip restore behavior; saved `.mem` payloads now also avoid persisting `PUBLIC` scope markers when the visible binding is actually a shadowing `LOCAL` or `PRIVATE` over an outer public name
- `RELEASE` — first-pass public/global release semantics now preserve public-pinned scalar variables and arrays across `RELEASE ALL`, `RELEASE ALL LIKE`, and `RELEASE ALL EXCEPT`, while named and non-public release paths continue to remove ordinary bindings; releasing current-frame `LOCAL` names now also reveals shadowed outer bindings instead of erasing them
- `SELECT`
- `USE`, `USE IN`, `USE AGAIN`
- `SET DATASESSION TO`
- `OPEN DATABASE`, `SET DATABASE TO`, `CLOSE DATABASE(S) [ALL]` — first-pass immutable DBC lifecycle parity now tracks open/current database identity per data session, preserves first-open `EXCLUSIVE` / `SHARED` plus `NOUPDATE` state, resolves omitted `.dbc` extensions through the default directory and `SET PATH`, and resolves DBC/DCT/DCX casing deterministically on case-sensitive hosts; `CLOSE DATABASE(S)` closes the current database, `CLOSE DATABASES ALL` closes databases in the current data session, and `CLOSE ALL` releases database state across data sessions
- `DBC()` / `DBUSED(cDatabaseName)` — first-pass current-database path and current-data-session open-database lookup; no undocumented `DATABASE()` alias is exposed
- `SET DEFAULT TO`
- `SET` — first-pass runtime-state surface now includes second-hop holder macro value coverage across ordinary string/numeric/logical options in addition to the earlier direct-variable paths; focused regressions now lock down representative forms such as `SET PATH TO &cPathTargetDeepHolder`, `SET MARK TO &cMarkTargetDeepHolder`, `SET DECIMALS TO &cDecimalsTargetDeepHolder`, `SET COLLATE TO &cCollateTargetDeepHolder`, `SET NULL TO &cNullTargetDeepHolder`, and `SET ANSI TO &cAnsiTargetDeepHolder`
- `CLOSE ALL` / `CLOSE TABLES` / `CLOSE DATABASES` — first-pass close semantics now cover open work-area cursors, session-scoped DBC lifecycle state, and non-DBF runtime handles for `ALL`/`DATABASES` scopes (SQL connection state, tracked OLE handles, and outstanding low-level `FOPEN` handles)
- `ON ERROR`
- `ON SHUTDOWN` — first-pass shutdown-handler semantics for `ON SHUTDOWN DO <routine> [WITH ...]` plus common inline compatibility forms including `ON SHUTDOWN CLEAR EVENTS` and close-style cleanup commands such as `ON SHUTDOWN CLOSE DATABASES ALL`; shutdown routines run before final `QUIT` completion and nested `QUIT` inside the shutdown routine is treated as the terminal quit rather than recursing indefinitely
- `PUBLIC` — first-pass public identity declaration for memory variables and arrays
- `LOCAL`
- `PRIVATE`
- `PARAMETERS` / `LPARAMETERS` — first-pass argument binding for `DO ... WITH ...`, including `@` by-reference write-back and default-value expressions when callers omit trailing arguments; default expressions now evaluate through the ordinary expression engine, so macro-expanded defaults such as `LPARAMETERS x = &cExpr` resolve consistently; routine declarations that carry inline signatures such as `PROCEDURE Foo(x)` and `FUNCTION Bar(x, y)` now also normalize the routine name and materialize an implicit `LPARAMETERS` declaration so inline parameter forms bind through the same stack-frugal call path as explicit `LPARAMETERS`
- `DIMENSION` / array-form `DECLARE` — first-pass runtime arrays with one- and two-dimensional sizing
- `DECLARE ... IN <dll>` — first-pass Windows native / managed declaration surface, including bare `@var` call arguments and native by-ref write-back for declared `STRING @` and numeric parameters when the callsite also uses `@`; mounted VFP9 documentation says omitting either marker passes by value, but Copperfin deliberately rejects a declared numeric `@` without a call-site `@` variable as localized Error 11 before native entry rather than reproducing the arbitrary-pointer crash hazard. Managed declarations currently target public static .NET Framework v4 methods named as `Namespace.Type.Method`, load the exact resolved assembly through `Assembly.LoadFrom`, retain CLR binder scalar coercion, resolve sibling dependencies, and preserve native-export precedence for mixed-mode images; managed objects/events/callbacks/by-reference write-back and non-Windows managed hosting remain outside this first-pass surface.
- `STORE` — evaluates the expression once and assigns through the same target semantics as ordinary assignment, including scalar variables, direct array elements, macro-expanded targets, `m.` memory variables, and OLE property targets
- `CREATE TABLE` — first-pass local DBF creation for field declarations (`C`/`N`/`F`/`B`/`L`/`D`/`I`/`Y`/`T`/`M`/`V`/`Q` plus common long-form names); nullable/default annotations are accepted by the parser, created/open cursors apply field defaults for omitted `INSERT INTO` fields, and `NOT NULL` fields are validated with append rollback on failure. Free-table physical field names are limited to 10 encoded bytes and are rejected through localized diagnostics before DBF/FPT publication.
- `CREATE CURSOR` — local temp-backed DBF cursor creation using the shared structural field-declaration parser and field-rule mapping; optional `NAME <alias>` declarations open under the explicit alias rather than combining the base cursor name and clause text. Resulting cursors participate in normal local-table mutation/schema flows (`APPEND BLANK`, `REPLACE`, `INSERT INTO`, `AFIELDS`, `FCOUNT`, `FSIZE`, `RECCOUNT`) instead of the old synthetic stub, and `DEFAULT` / `NOT NULL` metadata are enforced through the same open-cursor field-rule path used by `CREATE TABLE`
- `ALTER TABLE ... ADD/DROP/ALTER COLUMN` — first-pass local DBF schema rewrite that appends, removes, or rewrites one supported field while preserving row values and deleted flags where the resulting schema can store them; `ADD COLUMN ... DEFAULT ...` now also backfills existing rows with the declared default instead of leaving the new field blank
- structural rollback coverage now explicitly proves that `ALTER TABLE` rewrites and `PACK MEMO` compaction remain readable after `ROLLBACK`, so the structural lane is defended against transaction-unwind regressions as well as forward-mutation success
- `COPY TO` — full local DBF export via `create_dbf_table_file`; optional `FIELDS` clause filters columns, including single-field keyword-named selections such as `FIELDS TYPE` plus deeper `LIKE` / `EXCEPT` forms across the same copy-to/copy-to-array lane; optional `FOR` clause filters rows by visibility; first-pass `TYPE SDF` fixed-width text export plus VFP-style `TYPE CSV`, `TYPE TAB`, and `DELIMITED` text export, including CSV field-name headers, quoted character fields, tab-delimited rows, `WITH <enclosure>`, and `WITH CHARACTER <separator>` handling; first-pass `TYPE JSON` export emits a modern object-array JSON payload with typed numeric/logical values and string keys from field names; first-pass `TYPE XLS` export emits SpreadsheetML-style workbook XML with header-row and typed cell data; first-pass `TYPE DIF` export emits a conservative DIF-style text table with header-row emission and typed numeric/string cells; first-pass `TYPE SYLK` export emits a conservative SYLK-style text table with header-row emission and typed numeric/string cells; first-pass `COPY TO ARRAY <name>` populates a two-dimensional runtime array (rows=qualifying records, cols=fields) with optional `FIELDS` and `FOR` clauses, including macro-expanded array-name targets and true second-hop holder forms such as `&cMacroArrayDeepHolder`, `&cArrayNameDeepHolder`, and `&cTempArrayDeepHolder`
- selected synthetic SQL/result cursors now also materialize DBF output for plain `COPY TO` even when no backing local source path exists, deriving schema from remote field metadata (or remote rows) and preserving selected alias/record pointer state during export
- wildcard `FIELDS LIKE` / `FIELDS EXCEPT` coverage now explicitly reaches DBF `COPY TO` / `APPEND FROM` flows in addition to the earlier array-transfer and browse/name-object slices
- DBC metadata export now also has a first-pass whole-database JSON lane through the native asset-inspector API (`export_database_as_json`), including decoded binary `PROPERTIES` memo bags from `.dct` sidecars, case-insensitive same-base `DCT/DCX/DBF` resolution on case-sensitive filesystems, preference for the catalog `DATABASE` object name over the raw `.dbc` stem, and table-schema/table-row snapshots for resolvable local `TABLE` catalog entries
- `COPY STRUCTURE TO` — schema-only export (zero records); first-pass
- selected SQL/result cursors now also export schema-only DBF output through `COPY STRUCTURE TO`, including metadata-only remote cursors with zero rows. Logical metadata names longer than the free-table 10-byte physical limit are mapped deterministically and collision-safely for DBF output without changing the selected cursor's logical schema; already-valid physical names remain unchanged.
- `APPEND FROM` — full local DBF import via `parse_dbf_table_from_file`; inserts each non-deleted source record using `append_blank_record_to_file` + `replace_record_field_value`; when the source DBF is already open, its active `SET FILTER` expression selects import rows without changing the source cursor position; optional `FIELDS` clause, including single-field keyword-named selections such as `FIELDS TYPE` plus deeper `LIKE` / `EXCEPT` forms across the same append/import array lane; native DBF name matching continues to skip source-only fields but now aborts and atomically restores DBF plus cursor state when a matched destination-field write fails; first-pass `TYPE SDF` fixed-width text import plus first-pass `TYPE CSV`, `TYPE TAB`, and `DELIMITED` import with quoted-field parsing, tab-delimited rows, and matching CSV header skipping; first-pass `TYPE JSON` import reads the shipped object-array JSON lane and maps object keys to destination field names through the shared blank-row plus field-replacement path; first-pass `TYPE XLS` import reads the shipped SpreadsheetML-style workbook lane, skips a matching header row, and appends rows through the shared blank-row plus field-replacement path; first-pass `TYPE DIF` import reads the shipped DIF-style text lane, skips a matching header row, and appends rows through the same shared DBF mutation path; first-pass `TYPE SYLK` import reads the shipped SYLK-style text lane, skips a matching header row, and appends rows through that same shared DBF mutation path; failed file-based format imports now share DBF/cursor rollback through the common command-undo guard; first-pass `APPEND FROM ARRAY <name>` appends rows of a runtime array as new records (columns map positionally to destination fields with optional `FIELDS` filtering), including macro-expanded array-name sources, true second-hop holder forms such as `&cTempArrayDeepHolder` and `&cArrayNameDeepHolder`, typed date/datetime serialization instead of string-only writes, and command-atomic rollback with restored cursor state when any matched destination-field write fails
- selected synthetic SQL/result cursors now also support first-pass DBF `APPEND FROM` mutation in-memory (field-name mapping plus `FIELDS` filtering) instead of event-only stubbing, while unsupported non-DBF source types return deterministic runtime diagnostics
- selected synthetic SQL/result cursors now also apply `FOR` filtering during DBF `APPEND FROM` mutation, so only rows matching the clause are materialized into the target cursor; focused SQL cursor macro-filter coverage now also includes true second-hop `FOR &expr` forms across `LOCATE`, `DELETE`, and `RECALL`, such as `&cLocateExprDeepHolder` and `&cDeleteExprDeepHolder`
- selected synthetic SQL/result cursors now also support `APPEND FROM TYPE JSON` and `APPEND FROM TYPE CSV` / `TYPE DELIMITED` in-memory mutation: JSON sources map object keys to remote cursor field names, CSV/DELIMITED sources map positional values with optional CSV header-row skip; both branches honor `FIELDS` filtering and the `FOR` clause; previously both paths returned a "DBF source only" stub error
- `SCATTER MEMVAR` / `SCATTER TO <array>` / `SCATTER NAME <object>` — snapshot current record fields to `m.<fieldname>` variables, runtime arrays, or object properties; `FIELDS`, `BLANK`, and `MEMO` are honored with typed values, including runtime-facing `MM/DD/YYYY` and `MM/DD/YYYY HH:MM:SS` strings for `D`/`T` fields; the parser now also disambiguates single-field filters such as `FIELDS NAME` from the command's own `NAME` clause for both memvar and object-target forms, and deeper regression coverage now exercises `LIKE` / `EXCEPT` filters on object-target flows; macro-expanded array-name targets are supported, including direct true second-hop holder forms such as `SCATTER TO &cMacroRowNameDeepHolder`; `SCATTER NAME &cObjectName`-style macro-expanded object variable targets are also supported, including direct true second-hop holder forms such as `SCATTER NAME &cObjectNameDeepHolder`; nested object-property targets such as `SCATTER NAME oHolder.Row` / `GATHER NAME oHolder.Row` are supported, including macro-expanded nested path segments such as `SCATTER NAME &cHolder.&cChild` and direct true second-hop holder forms such as `SCATTER NAME &cHolderDeepHolder.&cChildDeepHolder`; scatter now creates missing nested child-object targets on demand instead of requiring them to already exist; non-`ADDITIVE` scatter on an existing object target now replaces that target object instead of mutating it in place, while `SCATTER NAME <object> ADDITIVE` reuses an existing object when present and merges field properties onto it; first-pass
- `GATHER MEMVAR` / `GATHER FROM <array>` — write `m.<fieldname>` variables or array elements back to current record via direct `replace_record_field_value` calls (type-faithful, no string-quote round-trip); `FIELDS` and `FOR` are honored, including single-field `FIELDS NAME` filters on both memvar and `GATHER NAME <object>` forms plus deeper `EXCEPT` coverage on object-target flows, date/datetime/blank runtime strings now restore consistently into DBF storage, and macro-expanded array-name sources are supported, including direct true second-hop holder forms such as `&cMacroRowNameDeepHolder`, while `GATHER NAME <object>` macro object targets now also have direct true second-hop proof via forms such as `&cObjectNameDeepHolder`; first-pass
- wildcard `FIELDS LIKE` / `FIELDS EXCEPT` coverage now also explicitly defends the MEMVAR scatter/gather path instead of only the NAME-object and array-transfer paths
- `SCATTER MEMVAR BLANK` (and other BLANK forms) now succeeds on zero-record tables by deriving typed blank values from the cursor schema instead of requiring a current record; `blank_value_for_field` now correctly returns an empty string for character ('C') and varchar ('V') fields, matching VFP behavior where `SCATTER BLANK` creates `''` for character-type memvars
- `UPDATE` — first-pass local/remote cursor command forms, including `UPDATE <alias> SET ...`, `UPDATE SET ...` against the selected cursor, and `UPDATE IN <alias> SET ...`; `WHERE`/`FOR` scope clauses reuse scoped record replacement semantics, including macro-expanded `FOR &cExpr` filters
- `DELETE FROM` / `INSERT INTO` — first-pass SQL-style local/remote cursor mutation forms; `DELETE FROM <target> WHERE/FOR <expr>` tombstones matching target rows and defaults to all rows when no condition is supplied, including macro-expanded `FOR &cExpr` filters and true second-hop local forms such as `FOR &cDeleteExprDeepHolder`; `INSERT INTO <target> [(fields)] VALUES (...)` appends through the shared blank-row plus field-replacement path, including expression-driven cursor targets, schema-order mapping when the field list is omitted, selected-cursor preservation, local DBF persistence, and synthetic SQL result cursor parity; `INSERT INTO <target> [(fields)] SELECT ...` now materializes through the same first-pass single/joined-cursor query planner used by native list-control `Requery()`, preserves selected/source cursor state, and rolls the complete append batch back if any target row fails
- `INSERT INTO` failure rollback — local and synthetic remote inserts now restore the previous record count when a field write fails after the blank-row append step, preventing a failed insert from leaving an extra blank row behind
- `PACK`, `PACK MEMO`, and `ZAP` — first-pass local DBF table-maintenance commands; `PACK` physically removes deleted records while preserving schema and kept-row order, `PACK MEMO` rewrites the memo sidecar from current memo values to compact stale memo blocks, and `ZAP` truncates the table to zero records while keeping it appendable; local cursors now honor first-pass exclusive-use guards from `SET EXCLUSIVE` / `USE ... SHARED|EXCLUSIVE`; synthetic remote/result cursors get matching in-memory behavior where applicable
- `SET REPROCESS`, `SET MULTILOCKS`, `RLOCK()`, `FLOCK()` / `LOCK()`, `ISRLOCKED()`, `ISFLOCKED()`, and `UNLOCK` — first-pass in-process lock-state tracking for open local/runtime cursors, including `SET()` readback, `UNLOCK ALL`, record-specific `UNLOCK RECORD <n>` release, `AUTOMATIC` default `REPROCESS` semantics, and bounded retry/timeout behavior across explicit lock attempts plus shipped local mutation paths; this establishes runtime-visible lock semantics but does not yet claim cross-process OS locking or transaction journaling
- `SET` runtime-state commands — first-pass normalized/evaluated handling for common state options including boolean options, `REPROCESS`, date/time formatting controls, `PATH`, `DECIMALS`, `COLLATE`, `MARK`, `POINT`, `SEPARATOR`, and `CURRENCY`; string/numeric RHS values can come from literals, variables, or macro-expanded expressions where supported, with `SET()` readback defaults for the shipped options
- `BEGIN TRANSACTION`, `END TRANSACTION`, `ROLLBACK`, and `TXNLEVEL()` — first-pass transaction nesting counters with per-data-session isolation, runtime event emission (`runtime.transaction.begin` / `runtime.transaction.end` / `runtime.transaction.rollback`), durable local DBF preimage journaling for shipped mutation paths, rollback cursor refresh/closure when replay removes a table, and startup replay of pending journals after interrupted transactions

Local DBF `APPEND BLANK` now also has a conservative opaque-field lane for otherwise readable unsupported record layouts: unknown/binary field bytes are zero-initialized instead of rejecting the whole append, which lets runtime mutation continue over mixed-layout tables while still avoiding overclaimed write-surface semantics for direct typed replacement on those field families.

Shared local DBF direct writes now also have a matching conservative opaque-field replacement lane for those same otherwise readable unsupported families: callers can write `NULL`, width-fitting raw text, or `0x`-prefixed hex payloads without inventing higher-level typed semantics for the underlying field family.

Command-level aggregate assignment now also includes first-pass `TO ARRAY` forms for `COUNT`, `SUM`, and `AVERAGE`, using one-dimensional runtime array assignment through the shared array helper path. Focused regression coverage validates scope-clause behavior (`ALL`/`REST`/`NEXT`/`RECORD`), `FOR`/`WHILE` filtering, `IN` alias targeting, `TO &macro` target resolution on the command aggregate lane including second-hop holder forms such as `&cCountHolder`, and malformed `TO ARRAY` diagnostics. `CALCULATE` now also preserves `WHILE` clause semantics on the same aggregate path. Aggregate expression parity also now includes omitted-expression forms: `COUNT()` returns visible-row counts, and no-expression `SUM()` / `AVG()` / `MIN()` / `MAX()` use the first numeric-compatible field (`N/F/B/I/Y`) with deterministic no-numeric-field fallbacks.

The `m.` variable namespace prefix is also now correctly handled as a memory-variable alias across assignment, lookup, expression evaluation, and declarations: `m.<name>`, `M.<name>`, and bare `<name>` share the same local/global runtime binding, and `m.<name>` no longer falls through to OLE property resolution.

The memory-variable lane now also restores saved outer bindings immediately when `RELEASE <name>` targets a currently shadowing `PRIVATE` variable, instead of waiting until the current frame unwinds.

`PRIVATE ALL [LIKE <skeleton> | EXCEPT <skeleton>]` now hides matching existing caller variables and arrays using VFP `?`/`*` wildcard matching, restores their scalar and array state on explicit release or frame unwind, and leaves nonmatching `LIKE` / matching `EXCEPT` bindings visible to the called routine.

Wildcard `RELEASE ALL LIKE ...` / `RELEASE ALL EXCEPT ...` handling now also covers array-only names rather than only scalar/global bindings, while continuing to preserve `PUBLIC` variables and arrays.

`CLEAR MEMORY` now also drops deferred `PRIVATE` saved bindings so cleared names do not reappear later when nested frames unwind.

`RELEASE ALL` now also clears current-frame `LOCAL` bindings instead of only global/array candidates, and later reassignment of those locals stays in local scope rather than leaking new globals.

`CLEAR MEMORY` now also clears current-frame `LOCAL` bindings while preserving local declaration routing for later in-scope reassignment.

The same memory-file lane now also has first-pass array/scope fidelity for `SAVE TO` / `RESTORE FROM`: saved `.mem` payloads now preserve runtime arrays with dimensions plus real visible-`PUBLIC` scope markers, additive restore now writes through visible `LOCAL` bindings instead of leaving restored values hidden behind stale locals, and non-additive restore now clears stale arrays and deferred `PRIVATE` shadow state before recreating the saved bindings.

That same memory-lifetime lane now also handles shadowed locals more faithfully: `RELEASE <name>` and `RELEASE ALL` remove current-frame `LOCAL` bindings without destroying the outer global/public value they were masking.

The indexed-search lane now also has a first-pass runtime collation step for plain string orders: when an active order does not already carry an explicit case-folding expression hint, non-`MACHINE` session collations such as `SET COLLATE TO GENERAL` now case-fold plain string seek comparisons instead of leaving `SET COLLATE` as metadata-only state.

That plain-string collation step is now explicitly defended on synthetic SQL/result cursors too: focused SQL seek coverage proves the same non-`MACHINE` session collation behavior on temporary `NAME`-style orders instead of leaving remote parity implied by the shared implementation.

The adjacent date/time runtime-state lane now also has stronger `SET()` readback fidelity: `SET('DATE')` defaults to `MDY` instead of falling through to `OFF`, and focused coverage now locks down data-session isolation/restoration for `DATE`, `CENTURY`, `MARK`, `HOURS`, and `SECONDS`.

The broader runtime-state lane now also has focused session-isolation/readback coverage for `COLLATE`, `NULL`, `ANSI`, `REPROCESS`, `MULTILOCKS`, and `EXCLUSIVE`, locking down their default values plus `SET DATASESSION` restoration behavior alongside the earlier shipped command semantics. The adjacent lock-contention slice now also proves that `REPROCESS` defaults to `AUTOMATIC`, that explicit numeric overrides bound retry counts deterministically, and that conflicting local lock/mutation attempts fail with timeout diagnostics instead of busy-spinning.

That same session-state lane now also explicitly defends formatting-state isolation/readback for `MEMOWIDTH`, `POINT`, `SEPARATOR`, and `CURRENCY`, plus adjacent calendar/week-state isolation/readback for `FDOW` and `FWEEK`. The shared `SET()` readback path also now preserves numeric/string values for these non-boolean settings instead of collapsing values like `1` to `ON` after session restoration.

Runtime arrays now have first-pass direct element reads and writes through both bracket and parenthesis syntax (`array[1]`, `array[1,2]`, `array(1)`, `array(1,2)`), including declared arrays and arrays resized through `ASIZE()`. Macro-expanded array identifiers now also work across adjacent helper and element-access paths such as `ALEN(&cArrayName)`, `ALINES(&cTargetName, ...)`, `ACOPY(&cSourceName, &cTargetName, ...)`, `&cArrayName[2,1]`, and `&cArrayName(1,2)`, and focused coverage now includes true second-hop forms such as `ALINES(&cLinesNameDeepHolder, ...)`, `ALEN(&cArrayNameDeepHolder)`, `AELEMENT(&cArrayNameDeepHolder, ...)`, `ACOPY(&cArrayNameDeepHolder, &cCopyNameDeepHolder, ...)`, `&cArrayNameDeepHolder[2,1]`, and `&cArrayNameDeepHolder(1,2)`. Shared assignment-target coverage now also includes true second-hop macro-expanded array-element targets such as `STORE 6 TO &cMacroTargetDeepHolder`. `parse_primary()` now also prefers shipped array-helper function calls over same-named runtime arrays when `(` follows an identifier, avoiding collisions such as an existing `aLines` array shadowing `ALINES(...)`. The array helper surface now also covers `ACOPY()` row-major copies over one- and two-dimensional arrays, `AELEMENT()` / `ASUBSCRIPT()` index conversion, `ASCAN()` start/count scans with column, case-insensitive, exactness, row-return, and first-pass predicate-expression search support, `ASORT()` start/count windows plus descending/case-insensitive/numeric-aware ordering and two-dimensional row sorting by the start element's column, and two-dimensional `ADEL()` / `AINS()` row/column shifting. Focused proof now also covers true second-hop target-array holder chains for `ALINES()` and `ACOPY()` via forms such as `&cLinesNameDeepHolder` and `&cCopyNameDeepHolder`. `ASCAN()` macro-expanded predicate fidelity and scan-metadata isolation are also now fully proven: variable-reference predicates, first-hop macro-expanded predicates, and second-hop holder-chain predicates all resolve correctly, and all four `_ASCAN*` metadata variables are restored on every exit path.

The current xAsset runtime surface behind those commands is now green at the first-pass runtime level: `DO FORM` is backed by generated `SCX/VCX` bootstrap sources that sequence `DataEnvironment` open/close hooks plus root-object lifecycle methods where present, `REPORT FORM` and `LABEL FORM` now both support preview/event-loop and `TO FILE` execution lanes, and menu assets now have executable startup/action/cleanup bootstrap modeling through the shared `MNX` xAsset path. The expression evaluator also accepts VFP square-bracket string literals, including doubled closing-bracket escapes, and routes dotted `m.`-qualified object paths through native property resolution. Dynamic `CREATE TABLE`/`INSERT INTO` table targets and DateTime storage are covered by the FOXREF startup slice under #3217/#4691; the packaged sample now completes, while broader FoxRef parity remains separate work.

The current indexed-search slice covers `SET ORDER TO TAG`, first-pass `ASCENDING` / `DESCENDING` direction control on the local `SET ORDER` / `SEEK` path, grounded `UPPER(...)` seek-key normalization on that same search path, first-pass composite tag-expression evaluation for concatenated keys such as `UPPER(LAST+FIRST)`, first-pass substring-style tag evaluation for `LEFT(...)`, `RIGHT(...)`, and `SUBSTR(...)` expressions such as `UPPER(LEFT(NAME, 3))`, `UPPER(RIGHT(NAME, 3))`, and `UPPER(SUBSTR(NAME, 2, 3))`, first-pass grounded `FOR`-filtered order behavior for extracted `DELETED() = .F./.T.` hints, command/function `SEEK`, `INDEXSEEK()`, `ORDER()`, `TAG()`, `SET EXACT`, `SET NEAR`, function-order designator suffix parsing for `SEEK()` / `INDEXSEEK()` one-off `ASCENDING` / `DESCENDING` overrides, command-level one-off `SEEK ... TAG/ORDER` probes for local DBF-backed cursors, first-pass ad hoc local order expressions such as `SET ORDER TO UPPER(NAME)` and `SEEK(..., 'People', 'UPPER(NAME)')`, and descriptive stored CDX tag names such as `FULLNAME` now bind to their hinted tag-page-local expressions even when the tag name itself does not resemble the key expression. The runtime also preserves the actual inspected index-file identity for `ORDER(alias, 1)` plus `TAG(indexFile, ...)` across supported xBase index extensions instead of assuming `.cdx`.

Indexed candidate visibility now composes cursor-scoped `SET FILTER` and session-scoped `SET DELETED` on that same local and synthetic SQL `SEEK`/`SEEK()`/`INDEXSEEK()` path. Hidden or deleted duplicates are skipped without changing exact/prefix, ascending/descending, `SET NEAR`, status-function, or `INDEXSEEK()` pointer contracts; `SET DELETED OFF` remains independent from each order's own `FOR` expression.

Deferred filter evaluation now also matches the mounted VFP9 empty-result boundary behavior for nonempty local and synthetic SQL cursors. After `SET FILTER TO .F.`, `GO TOP` moves to physical EOF with `RECNO() = RECCOUNT() + 1`, `BOF() = .F.`, and `EOF() = .T.`; `GO BOTTOM` retains that record number and reports both `BOF()` and `EOF()` true. `FOUND()` is preserved, and character/numeric field reads at either boundary return their schema-typed blank values. Local schema descriptors are cached and refreshed on cursor open, transaction replay, and schema mutation so these boundary reads do not reread DBF contents.

That local indexed-search slice now also evaluates a broader grounded tag-expression family on the shipped `SEEK` path, including `LEFT(...)`, `RIGHT(...)`, `SUBSTR(...)`, `PADL(...)`, `PADR(...)`, and a constrained first-pass `STR(<numeric>[, <width>[, <decimals>]])` path captured from loaded tag metadata, including first-pass default-space `PADL/PADR` behavior. The seek path now also preserves significant search-key whitespace so padded tag expressions can match without trimming away meaningful leading/trailing spaces.

Grounded order `FOR` metadata is now consumed a little deeper on the same seek path instead of staying mostly descriptive. In addition to the earlier `DELETED()` filter support, local indexed seeks now honor first-pass top-level string and numeric comparison filters such as `NAME = 'BRAVO'` and `AGE >= 20`, including correct `SET NEAR` miss positioning against the surviving candidate set.

That same local temporary-order path now also has focused command-path `IN <alias|work area>` parity coverage for ad hoc expressions such as `UPPER(NAME)`, including selected-alias preservation and descending `SET NEAR` behavior on non-selected targeted cursors. Coverage now also explicitly defends plain-expression targeted orders (`SET ORDER TO NAME IN People`) under session collation changes, proving that `SET COLLATE TO GENERAL` case-folds `SEEK(..., 'People', 'NAME')` while preserving selected non-target alias/pointer state. Synthetic SQL/result cursors now also honor first-pass ad hoc `FOR` filters on that temporary-order lane, so probes such as `SEEK(..., 'sqlcust', "UPPER(NAME) FOR NAME = 'BRAVO'")` and command-path `SET ORDER TO UPPER(NAME) FOR ...` constrain the candidate set and `SET NEAR` miss positioning instead of treating the `FOR` tail as inert text.

That same ad hoc-order lane now also consumes grounded field-type metadata for plain numeric field expressions such as `SET ORDER TO AGE` and `SET ORDER TO AMOUNT`, so local and synthetic SQL `SET NEAR` probes choose the next numeric key instead of falling back to lexicographic string ordering for one-off numeric temporary orders.

That same local ad hoc-order lane now also has focused helper-path parity for `INDEXSEEK(.F./.T.)` over temporary expressions such as `UPPER(NAME)`, including descending `SET NEAR` miss positioning while preserving the controlling `ORDER()` state.

The expression/function layer currently has first-pass support for these VFP-facing built-ins:

- `COUNT()`, `SUM()`, `AVG()/AVERAGE()`, `MIN()`, `MAX()`
- first-pass `ALEN()` for runtime arrays created by `SCATTER TO <array>`, `AERROR(<array>)`, `DIMENSION` / array-form `DECLARE`, and `ASIZE()`, including total element count and dimension queries
- deeper first-pass `AERROR(<array>)` with VFP-aligned seven-column rows for normal runtime errors: code, message, mixed-case error parameter, faulting work area, fault line, fault procedure, and failing statement text; SQL/ODBC-like `1526` rows now preserve captured handle/detail plus provider-or-operation context and command/target payload when the runtime has them, and OLE/automation-like `1429` rows now preserve captured member-path detail, source object identifiers, and failing action text instead of leaving trailing columns as placeholders when Copperfin already knows that payload
- first-pass runtime array helpers over Copperfin runtime arrays: `ACOPY()`, `ADIR()`, `AELEMENT()`, `AFIELDS()` over local and synthetic SQL cursor schemas, `ALINES()`, `ASIZE()`, `ASCAN()`, `ADEL()`, `AINS()`, `ASORT()`, `ASUBSCRIPT()`, and `AUSED()`; the focused array regression target now exercises predicate-style `ASCAN()` searches, including unquoted block-style predicate arguments, common two-dimensional `ACOPY()` row/column workflows, and declared-array/metadata helper coverage separately from the legacy catch-all PRG-engine test
- `EVAL()` and deeper first-pass bounded-indirection `&macro` substitution in expression paths (identifier-chain expansion with deterministic recursion/cycle safety), now including alias-qualified field continuations such as `&cAlias..NAME`, macro-driven suffix forms such as `&cAlias..&cField`, and focused nested evaluation coverage where stored expression text feeds another `EVAL()` layer
- `SQLCONNECT()`, `SQLSTRINGCONNECT()`, `SQLEXEC()`, `SQLDISCONNECT()`, `SQLROWCOUNT()`, `SQLPREPARE()`, first-pass `SQLGETPROP()`, `SQLSETPROP()`, `SQLCANCEL()`, `SQLCOMMIT()`, `SQLROLLBACK()`, `SQLDATABASES()`, `SQLPRIMARYKEYS()`, `SQLFOREIGNKEYS()`, `SQLTABLES()`, and `SQLCOLUMNS()`
- `CREATEOBJECT()`, `GETOBJECT()` — first-pass runtime activation parity now includes caller-PRG-first native class lookup followed by the ordered PRGs opened through `SET PROCEDURE TO ...
