That SQL slice now includes session-scoped synthetic cursor materialization that follows the current selected work-area flow for each data session when `SQLEXEC()` auto-opens a result cursor, plus first-pass connection-state helpers around that same per-session handle model: `SQLROWCOUNT()`, `SQLPREPARE()`, `SQLGETPROP()` / `SQLSETPROP()`, `SQLCANCEL()` / `SQLCOMMIT()` / `SQLROLLBACK()`, and first-pass catalog-style metadata helpers `SQLDATABASES()` / `SQLPRIMARYKEYS()` / `SQLFOREIGNKEYS()` / `SQLTABLES()` / `SQLCOLUMNS()`. The current connection-property pass now exposes deterministic `Connected`, `ConnectHandle`, `ConnectString`, `ConnectName`, `CurrentCatalog`, `LastCursorAlias`, `LastResultCount`, `Asynchronous`, `BatchMode`, `DispWarnings`, `DispLogin`, `Transactions`, `WaitTime`, and `PacketSize` behavior over the existing session-local handle state, while the metadata-cursor pass exposes deterministic synthetic database, table, primary-key, foreign-key, and column catalogs for top-level catalog enumeration plus `TABLE` / `VIEW` / `SYSTEM TABLE` probing, FoxPro-style and native-style `SQLCOLUMNS()` layouts, empty metadata cursors that still preserve schema, and strict cross-session handle rejection for the connection-state helpers. The shipped remote-cursor layer lets synthetic SQL rows participate in field lookup, targeted command-path filtering (`SET FILTER ... IN`) composed with targeted command-path navigation (`GO ... IN`, `SKIP ... IN`, `LOCATE ... IN`), command-path `SCAN ... IN` targeted iteration, aggregate built-ins, command-level `TOTAL` output aggregation, `CALCULATE`, in-memory `REPLACE` / `APPEND BLANK` / `DELETE` / `RECALL` mutation, non-selected-target `REPLACE ... IN` / `DELETE FOR ... IN` / `RECALL FOR ... IN` command-path behavior, `APPEND BLANK IN <alias|work area>` targeted append behavior with selected-alias/pointer isolation coverage, one-off `SEEK()` / `INDEXSEEK()` probes that use temporary order expressions over the in-memory result rows, first-pass normalization-aware temporary SQL order expressions such as `UPPER(NAME)` including combined `DESCENDING` suffix probes, and command-path `SET ORDER TO <expr>` / `SEEK` behavior over those same synthetic rows including non-selected-target `SET ORDER ... IN ... [ASCENDING|DESCENDING]` / `SEEK ... IN` parity, targeted backward/boundary navigation coverage (`GO BOTTOM IN`, `SKIP -1 IN`, targeted `BOF()` / `EOF()`), and post-mutation seeks against appended in-memory rows.

The current session/runtime-state slice also now keeps `SET DEFAULT TO` data-session-local, so `SET('DEFAULT')` and relative path resolution restore correctly after `SET DATASESSION` switches instead of leaking one session's default directory into another, and focused regression coverage now locks down the local-table counterpart to the shipped SQL selection-flow behavior so `SELECT 0`, `USE IN <selected-alias>`, and plain `USE` keep reusing each session's selected empty work area after session switches. `SET('FIELDS')` field-visibility readback and lookup restoration are now explicitly defended on that same data-session boundary too, so per-session `SET FIELDS TO ...` state does not leak across `SET DATASESSION` switches. Boolean runtime `SET` options used by search/filter semantics (`NEAR`, `EXACT`, `DELETED`) and runtime flags (`STRICTDATE`, `ENGINEBEHAVIOR`, `OPTIMIZE`, `TALK`, `SAFETY`, `ESCAPE`, `CENTURY`, `SECONDS`) now also normalize `SET ... TO` forms and logical token variants (`.T./.F.`, `1/0`, `true/false`, `yes/no`) to stable runtime state and `SET()` readback values across data-session switches, while `SET DATE TO`, `SET MARK TO`, first-pass `SET HOURS TO`, and first-pass `SET FDOW`/`SET FWEEK` state now feed the date/time helper parse/format path for common MDY/DMY/YMD orders, separators, time display, and week-number defaults.

`ON ERROR` now also has a richer first-pass compatibility lane: `ON ERROR DO <routine> WITH ...` can pass evaluated handler arguments, and handlers can inspect first-pass runtime fault metadata through `MESSAGE()`, `PROGRAM()`, `LINENO()`, `ERROR()`, `AERROR()`, `SYS(2018)`, and `ON('ERROR')` while execution resumes after the handler returns. Normal `AERROR()` rows now preserve the original faulting work area even if the handler changes selection before inspecting the array, handler execution now keeps those fault metadata helpers bound to the original fault even if the handler triggers and catches a secondary runtime error, and thrown-expression faults such as `LOG(-1)` / `ACOS(2)` now have explicit pause-state and repeated-fault coverage so line/statement/message metadata refresh correctly across multiple continues. Static PRG diagnostics are also now wired through the Studio document-open path so analyzer findings surface to editor-facing consumers instead of staying runtime-only.

`ON SHUTDOWN` now has a first-pass compatibility lane as well. `QUIT` checks for an active shutdown handler and, if configured, executes `ON SHUTDOWN DO <routine> [WITH ...]` before the final quit cleanup/unwind. Common inline compatibility forms are also recognized, including `ON SHUTDOWN CLEAR EVENTS` and close-style cleanup commands such as `CLOSE ALL`, `CLOSE TABLES`, and `CLOSE DATABASE(S) [ALL]`. `ON('SHUTDOWN')` now reports the active shutdown clause for runtime introspection. Shutdown handlers can still perform explicit cleanup such as `CLEAR EVENTS`, `CLOSE DATABASES ALL`, and even a nested `QUIT`; Copperfin treats that nested quit as the final terminal quit instead of recursively dispatching shutdown again.

The current PRG-engine slice now also supports first-pass `WITH/ENDWITH` member binding for leading-dot object access and first-pass `TRY/CATCH/FINALLY/ENDTRY` control flow, plus first-pass `DO ... WITH @var` reference semantics so `LPARAMETERS` assignments can write back into caller variables where needed for FoxPro-style procedure behavior. Error-pause stack metadata now also uses the actual faulting line for the top frame when nested routines fault instead of the post-incremented next statement. Inline `&&` comments now stay out of nested double-quoted and braced text during logical-line parsing, so macro-heavy expression text is less likely to be truncated before execution.

The official command inventory is much larger than the current runtime. The deepest next command groups should stay focused on data/runtime compatibility first:

- document/vector and AI planning policy: #30, #31

- `ASCAN()` macro-expanded predicate fidelity and metadata isolation are now closed: variable-reference predicates (`ASCAN(arr, cVarPred, ...)`, first-hop macro-expanded predicates (`ASCAN(arr, &cMacroPred, ...)`), and second-hop holder-chain predicates (`ASCAN(arr, &cPredDeepHolder, ...)`) all resolve to the correct block expression, and all four `_ASCAN*` metadata variables (`_ASCANVALUE`, `_ASCANINDEX`, `_ASCANROW`, `_ASCANCOLUMN`) are fully restored to their pre-scan state on every exit path including match, no-match, and early-return; focused regression coverage proves each of these paths in `test_ascan_macro_expanded_predicate_and_metadata_cleanup` (issue #391)
- continue moving new array regressions into `tests/test_prg_engine_arrays.cpp` / `test_prg_engine_arrays` instead of expanding the legacy `test_prg_engine.cpp` catch-all
- continue moving standalone expression/function regressions into `tests/test_prg_engine_functions.cpp` / `test_prg_engine_functions` instead of expanding the legacy `test_prg_engine.cpp` catch-all
- keep expression-function additions in reference-derived batches rather than one helper at a time; after shipping `FORCEEXT()`, `FORCEPATH()`, `CHRTRANC()`, adjacent date/time batches (`DOW()`, `CDOW()`, `CMONTH()`, `GOMONTH()`, `SECONDS()`, `MDY()`, `CTOT()`, `DTOT()`, `TTOD()`, `HOUR()`, `MINUTE()`, `SEC()`), the numeric trig/log helper batch (`SIN()`, `COS()`, `TAN()`, `ASIN()`, `ACOS()`, `ATAN()`, `ATN2()`, `DTOR()`, `RTOD()`, `LOG10()`), the first-pass phonetic string helpers (`SOUNDEX()`, `DIFFERENCE()`), the mixed utility batch (`MIN()` / `MAX()` expression lists, `STRCONV(..., 7/8)`, `ISLEADBYTE()`), and the path/color/random batch (`DEFAULTEXT()`, `CURDIR()`, `RGB()`, `RAND()`), continue with adjacent expression helpers from the reference inventory in similarly sized groups
- keep expression-function additions in reference-derived batches rather than one helper at a time; after shipping `FORCEEXT()`, `FORCEPATH()`, `CHRTRANC()`, adjacent date/time batches (`DOW()`, `CDOW()`, `CMONTH()`, `GOMONTH()`, `SECONDS()`, `MDY()`, `CTOT()`, `DTOT()`, `TTOD()`, `HOUR()`, `MINUTE()`, `SEC()`), the numeric trig/log helper batch (`SIN()`, `COS()`, `TAN()`, `ASIN()`, `ACOS()`, `ATAN()`, `ATN2()`, `DTOR()`, `RTOD()`, `LOG10()`), the first-pass phonetic string helpers (`SOUNDEX()`, `DIFFERENCE()`), the mixed utility batch (`MIN()` / `MAX()` expression lists, `STRCONV(..., 7/8)`, `ISLEADBYTE()`), the path/color/random batch (`DEFAULTEXT()`, `CURDIR()`, `RGB()`, `RAND()`), and the financial/misc batch (`HEX()`, `FV()`, `PV()`, `PAYMENT()`, first-pass `TEXTMERGE()` with configurable delimiters, first-pass `EXECSCRIPT()` RETURN-pattern detection, first-pass `LOOKUP()` seek-and-evaluate, first-pass `NEWID()` UUID v4, `CPCURRENT()` / `CPCONVERT()` / `CPDBF()` code-page stubs, and headless stubs `GETPICT()` / `GETCOLOR()` / `GETFONT()` / `VARREAD()`), continue with adjacent expression helpers from the reference inventory in similarly sized groups

### Automation And Compatibility Utilities

- Native PRG constructor and application-surface note: a no-return native `Init()` no longer inherits a nested routine's stale return value when deciding whether construction failed. The headless runtime also exposes `Application.Projects` as an empty read-only collection through the standard `Application` alias, allowing no-project startup paths such as VFPSource FoxRef to reach their own project-selection logic under #3217/#4691. The packaged FOXREF sample now completes through its startup/search flow; full FoxRef parity is not claimed.
- host stability and debugger fault containment are implemented for the current MVP runtime boundary: the process-boundary contract and PRG/form/class/menu/report/label debugger recovery regressions are covered by closed #4623/#4625/#4626/#4627; hosted Windows, mounted-VFP9, and Visual Studio validation remains release evidence under #4621

## How To Use The References

- Use the Learn language-reference index to decide what command/function families exist and to avoid missing major areas.
- Use the Learn general reference for file structures, table/index storage, and other low-level behavior.
- Use the generated local CHM command-topic index and topic manifest to jump from a command or `foxtools` function name to the installed VFP help page that describes its behavior.
- Use installed VFP 9 help and local source trees for product-era behavior and sample patterns.
- Use `FOXHELP.DBF` and its memo file as a fallback source for older semantics when Learn pages are missing, gated, or too shallow.
- Native visual `Caption` contract note — caption-bearing native PRG visual classes now expose a protected string `Caption` property with declarative initialization and direct/reflection coverage under #3217/#4457. TextBox remains outside this contract because VFP does not define `Caption` there; rendering and UI layout remain separate evidence lanes.
- Report/label `COMMENT` note: the VFP9 FRX/LBX schema uses memo-backed `COMMENT` fields for developer notes on report records. Copperfin now preserves non-empty root, band, and layout-object values with field/memo provenance and exposes localized editable values through the shared report/label model under #24/#4544; comments have no rendering, event, or runtime execution semantics. Reference: VFP9 FRX/LBX schema fixture in `/tmp/90frx.txt`.
- Report/label `USER` note: the VFP9 FRX/LBX schema reserves a memo-backed `USER` field for user comments and exposes it through the Report Builder interface; the Report Designer and Report Engine do not use its contents. Copperfin now preserves non-empty root, band, and layout-object values with field/memo provenance and exposes localized editable `User Comments` through the shared report/label model under #24/#4545; no rendering or executable semantics are implied. Reference: VFP9 FRX/LBX schema fixture in `/tmp/90frx.txt`.
- Report/label band `NOREPEAT` note: the VFP9 FRX/LBX schema uses band-level `NOREPEAT` for detail-band reprinting on each page, while report controls use the same field for remove-line-if-blank behavior. Copperfin now preserves band values with field/memo provenance and exposes localized editable values through the shared report/label section model under #24/#4546; rendering and report execution semantics remain separate. Reference: VFP9 FRX/LBX schema fixture in `/tmp/90frx.txt`.
- Native list-control `ProgrammaticChange` note: VFP9 documents this event for `ComboBox` and `ListBox` when code changes a control value, including `Selected` and `SelectedID` paths. Copperfin now dispatches it under #3217/#4547 after direct or reflective selection/value writes, list mutations, and `Requery()` only when the effective item/value signature changes; `InteractiveChange`, keyboard/mouse input, popup behavior, and pixel-level scrolling remain separate. Reference: installed VFP9 help topic `c229d522-dd27-4954-91bc-98ad00c19aea.htm`.
- Native list-control disabled-item colors note: VFP9 documents `DisabledItemBackColor` and `DisabledItemForeColor` for `ComboBox` and `ListBox` as integer color properties. Copperfin now exposes deterministic headless defaults and integer-normalized direct/reflection writes under #3217/#4548; per-row disabled state, rendering, theme translation, and UI color behavior remain separate. Reference: installed VFP9 help topic `0c022175-b166-4c55-9513-ac0aaa3bcf78.htm`.
- Native list-control item colors note: VFP9 documents `ItemBackColor` and `ItemForeColor` as read/write integer color properties on `ComboBox` and `ListBox`. Copperfin now exposes deterministic control-level defaults and integer-normalized direct/reflection writes under #3217/#4549; per-row color storage, rendering, theme translation, and UI color behavior remain separate. Reference: installed VFP9 help topic `4d140747-be2a-43bf-a897-b6a8b01ec119.htm`.
- Native list-control selected-item colors note: VFP9 documents `SelectedItemBackColor` and `SelectedItemForeColor` as read/write integer color properties on `ComboBox` and `ListBox`, with defaults derived from the Windows selected-item colors. Copperfin now exposes deterministic headless defaults matching the native TextBox selected-color fallback and integer-normalized direct/reflection writes under #3217/#4550; selected-row rendering, focus/highlight behavior, theme translation, and UI color behavior remain separate. References: installed VFP9 help topics `52f1bd74-b81d-4006-8bb0-f3fd6f108acf.htm` and `b931d352-1ba2-41b5-abd1-6b2c804c3c46.htm`.
- Report/label variable `TAG` note: the VFP9 FRX/LBX schema uses memo-backed `TAG` on `OBJTYPE = 18` variable records for the variable's initial value. Copperfin now preserves that value and field/memo provenance through the shared native/managed report model under #24/#4556 and exposes localized editable `Initial Value` while keeping invariant `TAG` in host JSON and update commands. Runtime expression evaluation, rendering, and Report Builder behavior remain separate. Reference: VFP9 FRX/LBX schema fixture in `/tmp/90frx.txt`.
- Native visual `HelpContextID` note: visual PRG classes use this numeric property as a help-context identifier. Copperfin now exposes it as a built-in property on native TextBox, EditBox, CommandButton, Form, and derived visual objects under #3217/#4558, with zero defaulting and nonnegative integer normalization across direct and reflective writes. OS help lookup, F1 routing, and UI rendering are not implied by this headless contract.
- Native visual `WhatsThisHelpID` note: visual PRG classes use this numeric property as a context identifier for WhatsThis help. Copperfin now exposes it as a built-in property on native TextBox, EditBox, CommandButton, Form, and derived visual objects under #3217/#4559, with zero defaulting and nonnegative integer normalization across direct and reflective writes. WhatsThis help lookup, F1 routing, and UI rendering are not implied by this headless contract.
- Native Form `KeyPreview` note: the mounted VFP9 `dv_foxhelp.chm` topic `html/9cb9ed7a-a8ab-445d-b534-8afc9b97fcd5.htm` documents this writable logical property for Form, Page, `_SCREEN`, and ToolBar, with `.F.` as the default and keyboard-event interception semantics. Copperfin now exposes the property on native PRG `Form` objects under #3217/#4637 with direct and reflective state coverage, derived-class preservation, and built-in reflection protection. Keyboard-event interception, Page/_SCREEN/ToolBar applicability, focus routing, and UI behavior remain separate evidence-backed work.
- Native Form `MDIForm` note: the mounted VFP9 `dv_foxhelp.chm` topic `html/aaa2b0c3-8dca-4b9e-8570-bbcbfa9b8bec.htm` documents this writable logical Form property with `.F.` as the default. Copperfin now exposes the property under #3217/#4674 with direct and reflective state coverage, derived-class preservation, and built-in reflection protection. MDI window arrangement, maximized-child behavior, and hosted UI behavior remain separate evidence-backed work.
- Native `NODEFAULT`/`KeyPress` boundary note: the mounted VFP9 `NODEFAULT Command` topic `html/906d0b2c-3c60-4c70-87b2-9e2cfc28d322.htm` specifies that `NODEFAULT` in `KeyPress` prevents the key press from entering the VFP keyboard buffer. Copperfin now dispatches modeled `WM_KEYDOWN` messages to the native object identified by the message `hWnd` under #3217/#4692, passes the key code and the encoded Alt-context bit (`nShiftAltCtrl = 4`), and returns a nonzero handled result when the method requests `NODEFAULT`; an ordinary key returns the modeled default result `0`. Shift/Ctrl state is not encoded in `WM_KEYDOWN` and remains a platform-adapter concern. This is a headless event/default boundary, not full keyboard-buffer, focus, `KeyPreview`, text-input, or UI parity.
- Native `Movable` note: the mounted VFP9 `dv_foxhelp.chm` topic `html/b58d37bb-48eb-47c1-805a-4e91d5e8996c.htm` documents this logical property for Column, Form, `_SCREEN`, and ToolBar, with `.T.` as the default and runtime writes disallowed. Copperfin now exposes the property on native PRG `Form` and `Column` objects under #3217/#4639 with direct/reflection read coverage, `PEMSTATUS()` read-only metadata, derived-class preservation, and built-in mutation protection. `_SCREEN`/ToolBar applicability, user dragging, Control-menu integration, column hit testing, and UI geometry remain separate evidence-backed work.
- Native `Anchor` note: the mounted VFP9 `dv_foxhelp.chm` topic `html/525f5ff6-eb50-4608-b63b-87b9b601f68d.htm` documents a writable bit-valued control property with default `0`, including conflicting absolute/relative and fixed-size combinations that must not be used. Copperfin now exposes the headless property on supported native visual controls under #3217/#4640, preserves valid combinations through direct and reflective writes, normalizes invalid values to `0`, and protects the built-in member from shadowing/removal. Form/ToolBar/Column applicability exclusions, host resize/reflow, designer layout, and platform pixel behavior remain separate evidence-backed work.
- Native `RightToLeft` note: the mounted VFP9 `dv_foxhelp.chm` topic `html/245df3b7-2dac-475a-9d7e-ccdd2eab01b5.htm` documents a read/write logical property with default `.T.` for CheckBox, ComboBox, CommandButton, EditBox, Form, Grid, Label, ListBox, OptionButton, PageFrame, Spinner, and TextBox. Copperfin now exposes that exact native PRG class boundary under #3217/#4641 with direct and reflective logical normalization, derived-class preservation, and built-in reflection protection. `_SCREEN` remains separate because its application-object dispatch path differs; host text layout, streaming output order, and platform rendering remain separate evidence-backed work.
- Native application `RightToLeft` note: the same mounted VFP9 topic applies the read/write logical property to `_SCREEN`, with `.T.` as the default. Copperfin now exposes shared direct dotted `_SCREEN.RightToLeft` / `_VFP.RightToLeft` state under #3217/#4643; `GETPEM()` / `SETPEM()` object-reference reflection, host text layout, streaming output order, and platform rendering remain separate evidence-backed work.
- Native `WordWrap` note: the mounted VFP9 `dv_foxhelp.chm` topic `html/5e0bd610-bd96-4fa6-ab73-9e4d5f13baae.htm` documents a read/write logical property with default `.F.` for CheckBox, CommandButton, Header, Label, and OptionButton, plus the `Grid.Column.Header` access path. Copperfin exposes the four control classes under #3217/#4642, explicit native `Header` objects under #3217/#4645, and lazily materialized `Grid.Column.Header` children under #3217/#4644. All three surfaces use direct and reflective logical normalization, preserve supported derived declarations, and retain built-in reflection protection. Focused Linux runtime-surface coverage and exact-head Windows validation for #4644 pass. Text measurement, wrapping, AutoSize interaction, caption layout, and rendering remain separate evidence-backed work.
- Native `Header.Caption` note: the mounted VFP9 help topic `html/066f6a98-132a-448b-9fae-c4f67cd9b75e.htm` documents `Caption` on the Header Object. Copperfin exposes an empty protected string default on explicit, derived, and lazily materialized `Grid.Column.Header` objects under #3217/#4646, with direct/reflective writes and declarative initialization covered by focused native regression tests; focused Linux and exact-head Windows validation at `78909f74` pass. Caption measurement, layout, and rendering remain separate evidence-backed work.
- Native `Header`/`Column` `Alignment` note: the mounted VFP9 help topic `html/8c351247-e41f-4bd2-8f0a-425ff25bdf29.htm` applies `Alignment` to Header and Column objects, documents Automatic default `3`, and defines the `0..9` state values; for Header objects, only `0..2` are effective when `WordWrap=.T.`. Copperfin exposes the headless property on explicit, derived, and lazily materialized `Grid.Column.Header` objects under #3217/#4647, with direct/reflective integer normalization and built-in reflection protection. Alignment rendering, text measurement, WordWrap interaction, and layout remain separate evidence-backed work.
- Native `Header`/`Column` color note: the mounted VFP9 help topic `html/124bcd5c-2771-419c-9518-64009bbb7e00.htm` applies `BackColor` and `ForeColor` to Header and Column objects. Copperfin exposes the properties on explicit, derived, and lazily materialized `Grid.Column.Header` objects under #3217/#4648 using the existing deterministic headless fallback (`BackColor=16777215`, `ForeColor=0`) with direct/reflective mutation and built-in protection. OS theme translation, color picking, painting, and layout remain separate evidence-backed work.
- Native list-control `BoundTo` transition note: Copperfin now preserves the documented bound-column `Value` while `ComboBox`/`ListBox.BoundTo` is true and restores one-based selected-index `Value` semantics after a true-to-false transition under #3217/#4561. Direct assignment and `SETPEM()` / `PUTPEM()` reflection are covered; this state contract does not claim popup, keyboard, or pixel-level UI behavior.
- Runtime Grid `LinkMaster` note: VFP9 documents a writable string property that identifies the parent alias used by a relational Grid. Copperfin now exposes a deterministic empty-string default for an unlinked headless native PRG Grid, preserves assigned alias text through direct access and `GETPEM()` / `SETPEM()` / `PUTPEM()`, and keeps built-in reflection and derived declarations coherent under #3217/#4675. Relation execution, child filtering, record-source navigation, visual refresh, hosted UI behavior, and designer serialization remain separate.

- Runtime Grid `ChildOrder` note: [VFP9 documents `ChildOrder`](https://vfphelp.com/vfp9/html/9fa79958-cbe1-458b-9720-f60162022bf5.htm) as the index tag used by a Grid or Relation object and read-only at run time. Copperfin now exposes a deterministic empty-string default for an unconfigured headless native PRG Grid, preserves declared tag text for reads/reflection and derived classes, and rejects runtime or dynamic-property mutation under #3217/#4676. Index selection, relation execution, child filtering, record-source navigation, visual refresh, hosted UI behavior, and designer serialization remain separate.

Expression evaluation, index selection, relation execution, child filtering, table refresh, hosted UI behavior, and designer serialization remain separate.

- Runtime Relation `OneToMany` note: the mounted VFP9 topic `html/ee0cd52a-1e92-4de0-8a49-8d9106900590.htm` documents a logical property with a `.F.` default that is read-only at run time. Copperfin now exposes the protected native PRG Relation property, preserves derived logical declarations, and rejects direct/PEM/dynamic mutation under #3217/#4678. SET SKIP, parent/child pointer movement, relation execution, table refresh, hosted UI behavior, and designer serialization remain separate.

- Runtime Grid `ScrollBars` note: the mounted VFP9 `ScrollBars Property` topic `html/27ab2417-d2e6-47e5-a577-cec12dbcad9d.htm` documents Grid values `0..3`, a `3` default, and read-only-at-runtime behavior. `VFPSource/builders/wbgrid.prg` also initializes a real builder Grid with `ScrollBars = 3`. Copperfin now exposes the protected native PRG Grid property with the documented numeric default, coherent reads/reflection, derived declarations, and rejected direct/PEM/dynamic mutation under #3217/#4679. Values accepted by the headless declaration path normalize to `0..3`; scrollbar painting, viewport measurement, keyboard/mouse behavior, hosted UI, and designer serialization remain separate.
- Runtime package `NEWOBJECT()` dependency note: literal second-argument library paths in staged PRG sources are discovered as project-contained package assets under #110/#4693, and supported VCX `METHODS` memo content is scanned recursively under #4695. Recursive scanning copies each worklist path before enqueueing further dependencies under #4697, preserving dependency discovery order without changing package contracts. VCX/VCT and other supported xAsset companions continue through the existing sidecar path. Native PRG and VCX activation uses ambiguity-aware case-insensitive local-path resolution on all platforms under #4696, preserving actual on-disk spelling for explicit PRG library provenance without changing manifest/debug keys or source-path fields. Compile-time `#INCLUDE` dependencies under #4698 now promote contained excluded headers when required, while both the package scanner and runtime parser accept common unquoted VFP syntax and resolve unique mixed-case paths; generated VCX bridges include resolved same-stem headers, preserving macro context without allowing arbitrary external loading. In strict verified sessions, admitted `.PRG`/`.H` bytes are exposed through parser source overrides and generated bridges resolve headers through the admitted original-path map, so physical tampering cannot replace the verified macro source; focused Linux and exact-head Windows coverage prove this boundary. The VFPSource ReportBuilder package stages the complete header chain and reaches the ReportBuilder event path without stack overflow in normal and debug launches; its separately caught `frxsetup.prg:250` operator/operand mismatch remains an explicit compatibility boundary, as does the local Linux `WIN32API`/`frxbuilder.prg:268` boundary. Dynamic library expressions, external COM/registered targets, and missing project assets remain separate boundaries; real VFPSource ReportBuilder stages and activates `_frxCursor.vcx`.
- Runtime `SET("DATASESSION")` note: VFPSource ReportBuilder saves the active session with `SET("DATASESSION")` before creating shared report-builder state and restores it through `SET DATASESSION TO (...)`. Copperfin now returns the active numeric session id for that query under #3217/#4694, so the generated VCX path no longer fails numeric conversion on `OFF`. The real sample advances to nested `_frxCursor.vcx` staging, tracked separately under #110/#4695; broader SET option parity remains separate.
